[1359] {#pg.105.1359}
πρὸς αἰωνίου ἐξάρπασόν με, μόνη Θεοχαρίτωτε·
Ἡ οὐρανώσασα ἡμῶν τὴν γεώδη καὶ φθαρτήν, ἁγνή, οὐσίαν, τῇ θερμῇ σου πρεσβείᾳ κατευθυνθῆναι ἡμῶν ἐντεύξεις καὶ δεήσεις ποίησον πρὸς τὸν σὸν καὶ πάντων Θεὸν καὶ βασιλέα.
Ἐξασθενήσασαν πάσαις προσβολαῖς τῶν κακίστων δαιμόνων τὴν ἀθλίαν μου ψυχὴν ἴασαι, ἄχραντε Δέσποινα, ἰατρὸν τετοκυῖα Χριστὸν τὴν ἀπολύτρωσιν ἡμῶν, τῶν εἰδότων σε κόρην Παρθένον ἀδιάφθορον.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς τρίτης τῆς αὐτῆς ἑβδομάδος.
Ἡ ἀγεώργητος ἡ γῆ ἡ βλαστήσασα τὸν τοῦ παντὸς Τροφέα τὸν ὑπανοίγοντα χεῖρα, καὶ εὐδοκίᾳ τῇ αὐτοῦ ἐμπιπλοῦντα πᾶν ζῶον ἰσχύϊ θείᾳ, στήριξον ἄρτῳ ζωτικῷ παρειμένας καρδίας τῷ κόρῳ τῶν δυσχερῶν ἡμῶν παραπτώσεων.
Θεογεννῆτορ ἁγνή, τῆς ψυχῆς μου τὰ ἕλκη καὶ καρδίας τὰ πάθη, καὶ νοὸς τὰς ἐκτροπὰς θεράπευσον, ὡς μόνη ἁμαρτωλῶν βοηθὸς καὶ πορθουμένων τεῖχος.
Τίς ἀκούσας ποιήσει τὰς αἰνέσεις σου τῆς τεκούσης ὑπὲρ νοῦν τὸν αἰνετὸν καὶ Δεσπότην, ἁγνή, καὶ Κύριον, ὃν ὑπεραινοῦσιν ἀγγέλων ταξιαρχίαι; Αὐτὸν οὖν ἐκδυσώπει ὑπὲρ λαοῦ ἡμαρτηκότος, Παρθένε ἀπειρόγαμε.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς τετάρτης τῆς αὐτῆς ἑβδομάδος.
Δεσπόζουσα ἁπάντων τῶν ποιημάτων, Δέσποινα, ὡς τὸν Δεσπότην τέξασα, τῆς δουλείας με ἐλευθέρωσον τοῦ δούλου τοῦ μόνου πολεμήτορος.
Σὲ τὴν φαεινὴν λαμπάδα καὶ λυχνίαν, ἐν ᾗ τὸ πῦρ τῆς Θεότητος οἰκῆσαν ἐφώτισε τοὺς νυκτώδει
finem; sequitur gloriosam Virginem plenam fuisse gratia in principio, medio, et fine; ergo concepta fuit absque peccato originali. Sic ille.
(44). Si diligenter evolvantur libri, disputationes, tractatus, et varia scripta eorum qui contra immaculatam conceptionem scripserunt vel eisdem modeste subscribere recusarunt, nusquam legetur apud illos titulus immaculatæ, toties a Josepho nostro attributus Virgini: nihil enim disputationem sustinentes volebant dicere repugnans, ut ab Aristotele in Topicis didicerant, et probe callebant titulum immaculatæ quomodocumque enuntiaretur, æque esse opinionis Virginem a peccato originali eximentis protestativum. Hinc Hieronymus ab Angesto, exacti judicii theologus, lectura 3, sui Propugnaculi contra nolentes gloriosissimam Virginem Mariam dici immaculatam, nullo alio pugnat argumentatione, quam probatione præservationis ipsius ab originali peccato, ratus non posse illi titulum immaculatæ competere, quam vel unius instantis macula fœdasset. Vide Armamentarium Seraphicum pro titulo immaculatæ Conceptionis pag. 153.
ab igne æterno eripe me, quæ sola es Dei gratia A plenissima (43).
Tu, o casta, quæ cœlificasti substantiam nostram terrenam et corruptibilem; ferventi intercessione tua fac ut dirigantur preces et supplicationes nostræ ad tuum atque omnium Deum et Regem.
Infirmatam omnibus pessimorum dæmonum invasionibus miseram animam meam, medica, o Domina immaculata (44), tu quæ medicum Christum redemptionem nostram peperisti: te enim, o Puella, Virginem incorruptam agnovimus.
TONUS II.
Ex canone feriæ III ejusdem hebdomadæ.
O terra, ab aratoris manu immunis, quæ uni- B versi Nutritorem produxisti, qui aperiens manum beneplacito suo, implet omne animal divina virtute: fertilica pane vitali corda nostra debilitata ac fracta ex satietate gravissimorum peccatorum nostrorum.
O Dei Genitrix casta, medicinam adhibe ulceribus animæ meæ, cordisque passionibus, ac mentis volubilitati; quippe quæ sola peccatorum es auxiliatrix, et murus eorum qui hostium deprædationibus sunt expositi.
Quis auditas faciet laudes tuas, o Virgo, quæ inexcogitabili modo peperisti laudabilem Dominatorem ac Dominum, quem primarii angelorum ordines collaudant. Eumdem tu deprecare, o Virgo C innupta, pro populo, qui sese peccatis obstrinxit.
TONUS II.
Ex canone feriæ IV ejusdem hebdomadæ.
Tu quæ in omnes creaturas exerces imperium, o Domina; quippe quæ Dominum ipsum peperisti, libera me a servitute dolosi omnium hostis.
Te refulgentem lampadem, et lucernam, in qua ignis Divinitatis commoratus illuminavit eos qui in
(43). Plenum (inquit Pelbartus in Stellario libr. IV, p. 1, q. 2, a. 3) est cujus nulla pars est vacua, IV, Phys. Ergo, subdit idem, pars originis, id est, conceptionis Mariæ, non fuit a gratia vacua, quod fuisset, si in peccato originali concepta foret; Sic Pelbartus. Dicitur porro B. Virgo a Josepho sola divina gratia plenissima: quia sola per singularem Dei in se benevolentiam, ab omni prorsus peccato, quod gratiam excludit, semper alienissima: vel quia sola a primo suæ conceptionis instanti, et per omne vitæ tempus, divinis oculis in omnibus gratiosissima: vel quia cum ejus gratia plenitudo sit ex ejus dignitate mensuranda et maternitatis ejus dignitatem omnem quamcumque hominibus possibilem dignitatem superet, plenitudo etiam gratiæ ipsius omnem puro Adæ filio communicabilem dignitatem excedet, et privilegium immunitatis ab originali peccato, quod gratiam excludit, in se includit. Hinc Paulus de Heredia in tract. De concept. B. V., ad Innocent. VIII, in illa, verba Ave Maria, gratia plena. Plenitudini, inquit, convenit, ut sit per totam essentiam subjecti, quia si ita non esset, nunquam plena esset: essentia vero subjecti divisa est in principium, medium, et

Ut verificetur hoc elogium, quod absolute Josephus tribuit Virgini Deiparae, nempe, quod ipsa dicatur tabernaculum immaculatum, requiritur, quod ipsa in nulla sui parte fuerit aliqua macula infecta; si enim aliquando maculata fuisset (quod sane accidisset si originalis peccati maculam contraxisset), non posset nec vere nec absolute dici tabernaculum immaculatum. O immaculata (46), quæ sola æternum Verbum ex dubio dicendum esset, si in primo suæ conceptionis, ac esse instanti aliquod peccatum veniale committeret. Ex, quo evidenter sequitur illam a Josepho attributam non posse vocari immaculatam si peccati originalis fœdaretur turpitudine. Nam si exigua peccati venialis macula impedimento foret in primo conceptionis instanti, quo minus immaculata nuncuparetur, quanto magis macula peccati originalis denominationem immaculatæ prohiberet? Maria, venerandum Domini receptaculum, erige nos collapsos in chaos gravissimæ desperationis, et peccatorum, et tribulationum: tu enim salus es peccatorum, et auxilium, et forte præsidium, et salvos facis servos tuos.
Mater boni Dei, bonam fac animam meam, quam Malignus mala consuetudine depravavit, misere inescans me: ut te causam salutis laudibus semper celebrem, quæ plurima laude es dignissima.
Peperisti, o Puella, sine viri opera, ab omni labe immunis, Deum, qui pugillo continet fines terræ, et omnibus est antiquior.
Patris sinu non derelicto, in sinu Virginis reclinatus es propter inclinationem humani generis, cujus similitudinem accepisti propter viscera misericordiæ tuæ: unde te homines laudamus in sæcula.
Tu, quæ gaudium concepisti ad vocem archangeli, abole nunc mærorem cordis mei, qui corrumpit animam meam: et da mihi, o. casta, luctum gaudii effectorem; ut in futuro sæculo divinam consolationem invenire possim.
Te gloriosam ac solam Deiparam, quæ Verbum incomprehensibile in tuo ventre continuisti, et secundum carnem peperisti, hymnis magnificamus.

Cum te, o Fili, ineffabili modo peperi; dolores partus effugi; nunc autem tota doloribus repleta sum: video enim te tanquam malefactorem in ligno suspensum, qui terram absque ullo fulcimento suspendisti. Ita castissima illacrymans loquebatur.
Cum vitam in cruce mortuam Virgo veneranda vidisset, cordis dolorem cohibere non valens, tota commovebatur et exclamabat: Hei mihi, Fili mi! quid tibi populus prævaricator et iniquus retribuit?
Ex utero virginali te peperi, o Fili, et nunc, videns te in ligno suspensum, conturbor et hæreo, et altitudinem mysterii, et multorum judiciorum tuorum profunditatem non comprehendo. Ita castissima exclamabat; quam nos vocibus incessabilibus tanquam Dei Matrem beatificemus.
Cum videres in cruce illum qui, propter bonitatem suam, ex te, o Immaculatissima, incomprehensibili ratione natus est, vulnerabantur viscera tua, ac dicebas: Hei mihi, divine Fili? quomodo pro omnibus dolore cruciaris? Pietatem tuam adoro; sit tibi gloria.
Da nobis vires, o casta, ut ab omni malitia jejunemus, et ut a pravis malisque actionibus semper abstineamus: virtutem nobis concede tu, quæ es omnium hominum præsidium.

Νῦν ὡς ἀρνίον ἄκακον καθορῶ σε κρεμάμενον, καὶ ὑπὸ ἀνόμων τῷ σταυρῷ πηγνύμενον, Υἱέ μου προάναρχε, τοῖς ὀδυρμοῖς συγκύπτομαι καὶ ταῖς μητρικαῖς περιστατοῦμαι ὀδύναις, ἡ πάναγνος ἐβόα, ἣν φωναῖς ἀσιγήτοις ὑμνοῦμεν καταχρέως, εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας. Φθορᾶς σε δίχα τέτοκα ἐκ γαστρὸς, πρὸ αἰώνων Πατὴρ ὃν ἐγέννησε, καὶ πῶς φθορεῖς ἄνθρωποι σπαράττουσι σε, Υἱὲ, καὶ τὴν πλευρὰν ὀρύττουσι λόγχῃ, καὶ τὰς χεῖρας σὺν τοῖς ποσὶν ἡλοῦσιν ἀπανθρώπως; ἡ πάναγνος ἐβόα, ἣν ἐπαξίως μεγαλύνωμεν. Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τοῦ Σαββάτου τῆς μέσης ἑβδομάδος, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον. Ὁ Θεοῦ Θεὸς Λόγος ζητῶν θεῶσαι τὸν ἄνθρωπον, ἁγνὴ, ἐκ σοῦ σαρκοῦται καὶ βροτὸς ὁρᾶται, ὃν ἀπαύστως δυσώπει, εὑρεῖν ἡμᾶς ἔλεος; ἐν καιρῷ τῆς ἀπολογίας. Σὲ τὴν μόνην ἀγαθὴν ἐκδυσωποῦμεν, Παρθένε, κεκακωμένους ἡμᾶς ἀγάθυνον, καὶ ἐκτενῶς τὸν Χριστὸν δυσώπει φύσει ὄντα ἀγαθὸν, ἐγκρατείας τὸν καιρὸν τὰ ἀγαθὰ πράττοντας ἀποπληρῶσαι ἡμᾶς ὑμνολογοῦντας αὐτόν. Τὸν σταυρὸν Χριστοῦ τὸν τίμιον, Θεοτόκε ἁγνὴ, βλέψαντας, καὶ ἐκ καρδίας προσκυνήσαντας ἡμᾶς, ταῖς σαῖς πρὸς τὸν Δεσπότην ἀξίωσον πρεσβείαις ἰδεῖν καὶ τὰ πάθη τὰ σεπτὰ αὐτοῦ, παθῶν κεκαθαρμένους. Σαρκός μου τὰς κινήσεις ἀπονέκρωσον, ἡ Θεὸν σαρκὶ ἀποτεκοῦσα, ὑπὲρ νοῦν, καὶ παράσχου φωτισμὸν τῇ διανοίᾳ μου, ἁγνὴ, νεφέλη οὖσα φωτός. Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς δευτέρας τῆς ἑβδομάδος ε´, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον. Τὴν ὡραιότητα τῆς παρθενίας σου, καὶ τὸ ὑπέρλαμπρον τὸ τῆς ἁγνείας σου ὁ Γαβριὴλ καταπλαγεὶς ἐβόα σοι, Θεοτόκε· Ποῖόν σοι ἐγκώμιον προσαγάγω ἐπάξιον; τί δὲ ὀνομάσω σε, ἀπορῶ καὶ ἐξίσταμαι. Διὸ, ὡς προσετάγην, βοῶ σοι· Χαῖρε, ἡ κεχαριτωμένη. Πολυώνυμε Κόρη, χαίροις, ἁγία Παρθένε, Θεοκυῆτορ Μαρία, τῶν πιστῶν καύχημα, κατάρας λύτρωσις, κλίμαξ ἐπουράνιε, ἀκατανόητον θαῦμα, βάτε ἀκατάφλεκτε, γῆ ἀγεώργητε. Ἡ μετὰ τόκον ἄφθορος μείνασα, πανάμωμε, λύτρωσαι, δεόμεθα, ἁγνὴ, καταφθορᾶς τοὺς δούλους σε πιστῶς τοὺς μελῳδοῦντας ἐν ὁμονοίᾳ ψυχῆς· Εὐλογεῖτε, τὰ ἔργα. Μετὰ μαρτύρων, μετὰ προφητῶν τὴν τεκοῦσαν σε, μετὰ ὁσίων πάντων, Χριστὲ, δυσωποῦσαν πρόσδεξαι ἀεί, ὑπὲρ δούλων τῶν παροργισάντων σε τὸν μόνον φιλάνθρωπον Δεσπότην, ὃν ἐπουράνιαι τάξεις τρέμουσι.


[1374] {#pg.105.1374}
stain viam feliciter deduc me, ut ad latitudinem vitæ perveniam, o Dei mater. Cum videam te nunc, o Fili æterne, tanquam agnum innocentem suspensum, et pro iniquis in cruce confixum, discrucior querelis, et doloribus maternis circumdor : clamabat castissima, quam vocibus assiduis debite laudamus in sæcula. Sine corruptione ex utero te peperi, o Fili, quem Pater ante sæcula generavit : et quomodo homines corruptibiles te dilacerant; et lancea latus tuum perfodiunt, et manus ac pedes inhumaniter clavis configunt? Ita castissima exclamabat : quam nos pro merito magnificemus. Ex canone in Sabbato mediæ hebd., ad Matutinum. Verbum Dei divinum volens hominem deificare, ex te, o casta, incarnatum est, et homo apparuit : quem sine cessatione deprecare, ut in tempore reddendæ rationis, misericordiam invenire possimus. Te solam bonam deprecamur, Virgo, ut nos malitia infectos, bonos efficias; et impense Christum exores, qui natura bonus est; quatenus abstinentiæ tempus in bonis operibus explere possimus, eumdem Christum collaudantes. Dum pretiosam Christi crucem intuemur, et ex corde adoramus; precibus tuis apud Dominum dignos nos effice, o casta Deipara, ut venerandas illius passiones a passionibus nostris purificati, intelligere possimus. Tu, quæ Deum in carne, intelligibili ratione peperisti, carnis meæ commotiones mortifica; et concede 317 menti meæ illustrationem, cum sis, o casta, nubes lucis. Ex canone in feria II Dominicæ v, ad Matutinum. Virginitatis tuæ pulchritudine, et immenso castitatis tuæ splendore obstupefactus Gabriel, clamabat ad te Dei para : Quo te encomio digne celebrare possim : et quo te nomine appellem prorsus ignoro, et mente excedo : propterea, juxta id quod imperatum est mihi, exclamo ad te : Ave, gratia plena. Salve, multis nominibus celebrata Puella; sancta Dei Genitrix Maria, fidelium gloria, maledictionis redemptio, scala cœlestis, miraculum omni intellectu superius, rubus incombustus, terra nunquam aratro proscissa. O purissima atque castissima, quæ post partum incorrupta permansisti, redime, te precamur, a corruptione servos tuos, qui fideliter concinunt unanimi consensu : Benedicite, opera, etc. Genitricem tuam semper suscipe, Christe, cum martyribus, prophetis, atque omnibus sanctis te deprecantem pro servis tuis, qui ad iracundiam provocaverunt te Dominum placabilissimum solum amatorem hominum, et omnipotentem, quem cœlestes exercitus contremiscunt.


[1375] {#pg.105.1375}
Α´ Ἐκ τοῦ κανόνος τῆς τρίτης τῆς ἑβδομάδος ε´, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Θεία γέγονας σκηνὴ τοῦ Λόγου, μόνη πάναγνε Παρθενομήτορ, τῇ καθαρότητι ἀγγέλους ὑπεράρασα, τὸν ὑπὲρ πάντας ἐμὲ βερυπωμένον σαρκὸς πλημμελήμασιν ἀποκάθαρον πρεσβείαις σου, τοῖς ἐνθέοις νάμασι παρέχουσα, σεμνή, τὸ μέγα ἔλεος. Ἐπανεπαύσατο Χριστὸς ἐν σοὶ τῇ μόνῃ εὐλογημένῃ, καὶ σάρκα τὴν ἑαυτοῦ ἐκ σοῦ ἐφόρεσεν. Τῶν οἰκτιρμῶν σου καὶ ῥανίσιν, Ἀειπάρθενε ἀμόλυντε, τοὺς μολυσμοὺς τοὺς ἐμπαθεῖς τῆς καρδίας μου ἀπόπλυνον, δακρύων ὀχετούς μοι παρεκτικούς καθάρσεως ψυχικῆς δωρουμένη. Ψυχὴν μεμὴν παθημάτων voluptuosarum nigredine obtenebratam, illumina, ο casta, quæ lucem peperisti, ut cum fide semper magnificem te.
Β´ Ἐκ τοῦ κανόνος τῆς τετάρτης τῆς ἑβδομάδος ε´, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Τὸν ἐξ αἱμάτων σου ἁγνῶν σωματωθέντα, καὶ ὑπὲρ ἔννοιαν ἐκ σοῦ, σεμνή, τεχθέντα, ἐπὶ ξύλου κρεμάμενον μέσον τῶν κακούργων ὁρῶσα, τὰ σπλάγχνα ἤλγεις, καὶ μητρικῶς θρηνοῦσα ἐβόας· Οἴ μοι, τέκνον, τίς ἡ θεία καὶ ἄφατος οἰκονομία σου, δι’ ἧς ἐζώωσας τὸ πλάσμα σου; ἀνυμνῶ σου τὸ εὐσπλαγχνον. Ἡ σωτήριος πύλη, ἡ πρὸς Θεὸν γέφυρα, σὺ Χριστιανῶν ἡ προστάτις, πάναγνε, Δέσποινα, περιστατούμενον ταῖς συμφοραῖς με τοῦ βίου καὶ κλυδωνιζόμενον, Κόρη, κυβέρνησον. Θεογεννῆτορ πάναγνε, τῆς ψυχῆς μου τὰ τραύματα, καὶ τῆς ἁμαρτίας τὰς οὐλὰς ἐξάλειψον, πηγαῖς ἀποσμήχουσα ταῖς ἐκ πλευρᾶς τοῦ τόκου σου, καὶ τοῖς ἐξ αὐτῆς ἀποκαθαίρουσα ῥείθροις· πρὸς σὲ γὰρ ἀνακράζω, καὶ σὲ καταφεύγω, καὶ σὲ ἐπικαλοῦμαι τὴν κεχαριτωμένην. Συνέλαβες, Παρθένε, δίχα σπορᾶς τὸν τὰ σύμπαντα δημιουργήσαντα Λόγον Θεοῦ ἄνευ θελημάτων τῶν τῆς σαρκός, ἄνευ φθορᾶς δὲ τέτοκας, ἄνευθεν ὠδίνων τῶν μητρικῶν· διό σε θεοτόκον καὶ γλώσσῃ καὶ καρδίᾳ ὁμολογοῦντες μεγαλύνομεν.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς παρασκευῆς τῆς ἑβδομάδος ε´, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Ὁρῶσά σε ἐν σταυρῷ ἠρτημένον ἡ παναγία, ἠλD λαζε τετρωμένη τὰ σπλάγχνα καὶ ἔλεγε· Διὰ σπλάγχνα, Κύριε, σῶν οἰκτιρμῶν παθεῖν ἠνέσχου, πᾶσι παρέχων ἀπάθειαν. Στάμνον τε τὸ θεῖον μάννα κεκτημένην τῆς Θεότητος ἔγνωμεν, Κόρη, κιβωτόν, καὶ τράπεζαν, καὶ λυχνίαν, θρόνον Θεοῦ, καὶ παλάτιον, γέφυραν μετάγουσαν πρὸς θείαν ζωὴν τοὺς ἀναμέλποντας· Εὐλογεῖτω ἡ πᾶσα κτίσις τὸν Κύριον. Πάντων ὑπερτέρα, Δέσποινα, τῆς τῶν παθῶν κακίας δεῖξον ὑπέρτερον τὸν δοξάζοντα παναληθῆ σε θεοτόκον, καὶ ὑμνοῦντα τὸν τόκον σου, ἄχραντε, τὸν ἀκαταληψία, θεοχαρίτωτε, τιμώμενον.
Ex canone in feria iii hebd. v, ad Matutinum.
Divinum Verbi tabernaculum facta es, o sola castissima Virgo Mater, quæ puritate angelos superasti; me super omnes carnalibus peccatis fœdatum emunda intercessionibus tuis, o veneranda; divinis irrigationibus præbens magnam misericordiam. Requievit in te Christus, o sola benedicta, atque ex te carnem suam suscepit. Miserationum tuarum stillicidiis, o semper Virgo inviolata, 318 passionum cordis mei sordes, emunda, donans mihi lacrymarum canales, quibus anima mea purificetur. Animam meam passionum voluptuosarum nigredine obtenebratam, illumina, o casta, quæ lucem peperisti, ut cum fide semper magnificem te.
Ex canone in feria iv hebd. v, ad Matutinum.
Cum videres, o veneranda, illum qui ex puris sanguinibus tuis corpus acceperat, atque ex te modo inexcogitabili natus fuerat, suspensum in ligno in medio malefactorum, visceribus discruciabaris, et materno affectu plorans clamabas: Hei mihi, Fili mi! quæ est hæc divina atque ineffabilis dispositio tua, per quam plasma tuum vivificasti? misericordiæ tuæ viscera collaudo. Porta salutaris, pons deducens ad Deum; Christianorum præses, Domina castissima; me vitæ calamitatibus circumdatum, fluctibusque jactatum, guberna, o Puella. O Dei genitrix castissima, animæ meæ vulnera, et peccati cicatrices abole, exsiccans atque exurens fontibus e latere Filii tui manantibus, et purificans fluentis quæ ex ipso procedunt: ad te enim vocem attollo, et ad te confugio, et te invoco gratia plenam. Concepisti, o Virgo, sine semine, eum qui universa creavit, Verbum Dei, absque voluntate carnis: absque corruptione peperisti, et absque doloribus, qui cæteras matres invadunt: propterea te Deiparam lingua et corde confitentes, magnificamus.
Ex canone in feria vi hebd. v ad Matutinum.
Cum videret te castissima in cruce sublatum, ejulabat corde vulnerata, ac dicebat: Propter 319 Urnam te, divinum Deitatis manna continentem agnovimus, o Puella; arcam, et mensam, et candelabrum, thronum Dei et palatium, et pontem ad divinam vitam traducentem eos qui concinunt: Benedicat omnis creatura Dominum, etc. O Domina universis sublimior, fac me passionum malitia superiorem, dum te verissimam Deiparam glorifico, et partum tuum hymnis celebro, o immaculata, Deo charissima, incomprehensibiliter honoratum.


[1377] {#pg.105.1377}
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τοῦ Σαββάτου τῆς ἑβδομάδος ε´, λ εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Tu quæ sola es omni laude dignissima, iis qui te remissio, et divinorum charismatum distributio, et
Ἡ τοῦ νόμου τὸν Δοτῆρα, Ἀειπάρθενε κυήσασα, ἱκέτευε τὰς ἀνομίας ὑπεξᾶραι πάσας ἐν καιρῷ τῷ νῦν ἐνεστῶτι τοῖς καλῶς προαιρουμένοις ἐξασκεῖν θείας νηστείας σπουδήν.
Ἱκεσίαις οἰκτίρμων τῆς κυησάσης σε, καὶ ἁγίων μαρτύρων, καὶ ἀποστόλων σου, φώτισον ἡμῶν τὰς ψυχὰς τοῦ δοξάζειν σε ἐν ἀγαλλιάσει ψυχῆς εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας.
Φωνῇ ἀγγέλου βουλῆς τὸν Ἄγγελον τῆς τοῦ Πατρὸς ἀρρήτως θεόν, Θεοτόκε, ἐκύησας· τὰς φωνὰς οὖν τῶν δούλων σου πρόσδεξαι, ἃς ἐν τῷ τῆς νηστείας χρήνῳ προσάγομεν, καὶ ταύτας προσάγαγε Θεῷ, ὥσπερ θυμίαμα.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς τρίτης τῶν Βαΐων, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Ἀρχὴ σωτηρίας ἡ τοῦ Γαβριὴλ προσηγορία πρὸς τὴν Παρθένον γέγονεν· ἤκουσε γὰρ τὸ, Χαῖρε, οὐκ ἀπέφυγε τὸν ἀσπασμόν, οὐκ ἐδίστασε ὡς ἡ Σάρρα ἐν τῇ σκηνῇ, ἀλλ᾿ οὕτως ἔλεγεν· Ἰδοὺ ἡ δούλη Κυρίου, γένοιτό μοι κατὰ τὸ ῥῆμά σου.
Χαῖρε, σοὶ βοῶμεν, τῇ κυησάσῃ τὴν χαράν, ἡ κεχαριτωμένη Θεοτόκε Παρθένε, Θεὸν ὃν ἔτεκες δυσώπει λυτρωθῆναι κινδύνων καὶ φθορᾶς τοὺς ἀνυμνοῦντάς σε ἀεί.
Παναγία Παρθένε, σύ με διάσωσον, τῆς ἐμῆς ἀσθενείας γενοῦ βοήθεια, ἡ τὸν θελητὴν τοῦ ἐλέους  κυήσασα, ὃν ὑπερυψοῦμεν εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας.
Ἐκ σοῦ, Παρθένε θεοχαρίτωτε, τοῖς ἐν νυκτὶ παθῶν κεκρατημένοις ἀνέτειλε τοῦ φωτὸς καὶ εἰρήνης Χριστὸς χορηγός, τὴν ἐξ ἀπροσεξίας λύων παράβασιν, καὶ τὴν ἀπολύτρωσιν ἡμῖν σαφῶς δωρούμενος.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος διπλοῦ τῆς τετάρτης τῶν Βαΐων, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Τῷ τιμίῳ σταυρῷ τοῦ Υἱοῦ σου φυλαττόμενοι, Δέσποινα ἁγνὴ Θεοτόκε, πᾶσαν προσβολὴν τοῦ πολέμήτορος ἅπαντες ῥᾳδίως ἐκτρεπόμεθα· διὸ σε καταχρέως δοξάζομεν ὡς Μητέρα τοῦ φωτὸς καὶ μόνην ἐλπίδα τῶν ψυχῶν ἡμῶν.
Μὴ φλεχθεῖσα τῷ πυρὶ τῆς θεότητος, ἁγνή, κατάφλεξόν μου τὰς ὑλώδεις ὁρμάς, τῶν παθῶν, ὅπως πιστῶς, ἁγνή, δοξάζω σε.
Παρθενομήτωρ ἄχραντε, ἡ ῥίζα τοῦ Ἰεσσαί, ἐξ ἧς Χριστὸς ἀνέτειλε, τὸ ζωηφόρον ἄνθος τῶν γηγενῶν, δι᾿ ἧς ἐλυτρώθημεν φθορᾶς καὶ θνητότητος, σὲ ὑμνοῦμεν πάντες, ἁγνή.
Ἐν σοί, Παρθένε ἁγνή, ἐθεωρήθησαν πᾶσαι τοῦ Θεοῦ αἱ πορεῖαι, τοῦ φυλάξαντος τὴν σὴν ἁγνείαν μετὰ τόκον ἐσφραγισμένην σαφῶς εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας.
Ἰδοὺ πᾶσαι γενεαὶ μακαρίζομέν σε, ἄχραντε, τὰ μεγαλεῖα τὰ σὰ καθορῶντες· σὺ γὰρ τίκτεις ὑπερφυῶς τὸν τοῦ κόσμου παντὸς Ποιητήν, Θεὸν ὄντα καὶ βροτόν.


[1378] {#pg.105.1378}
MARIALE.
Ex canone in Sabbato hebd. v, ad Matutinum.
fideliter laudant, precare ut concedatur peccatorum Verbum sanctissimum exora.
Virgo perpetua, quæ legis Datorem genuisti, eumdem precare, ut e medio tollat omnes iniquitates eorum, qui præsenti tempore in divino jejunio sese sollicite exercere deliberant.
Deprecationibus Genitricis tuæ, o misericors, et sanctorum martyrum et apostolorum tuorum, illumina animas nostras, ut glorificemus te in exsultatione animi per sæcula universa.
Ad vocem angeli Angelum consilii Patris ineffabiliter concepisti, o Deipara : voces ergo suscipe servorum tuorum, quas jejunii tempore offerimus, easque tu Deo offer, instar thymiamatis.
Ex canone in feriam iii in Ramis Palmarum, ad Matutinum.
Principium salutis fuit allocutio Gabrielis ad Virginem : audivit enim, Ave. Non refugit salutationem; non dubitavit ut Sarra in tabernaculo; sed ita dixit : « Ecce ancilla Domini, fiat mihi secundum verbum tuum. »
At te clamamus, Gaude, quæ gaudium concepisti, o gratia plena Deipara Virgo : Deum, quem peperisti, deprecare, ut liberet a periculis et corruptione eos qui te assidue concelebrant.
Virgo sanctissima, tu me salvum fac, et meæ infirmitatis esto auxilium, quæ misericordiæ amatorem concepisti, quem superexaltamus in omnia sæcula.
Ex te, o Virgo Deo charissima, iis qui in nocte passionum detinebantur, exortus est auctor lucis et pacis Christus, transgressionem ex negligentia ortam diluens, et manifeste nobis redemptionem dilargiens.
Ex canone duplici in feria iv in Ramis Palmarum, ad Matutinum.
Pretiosa Filii tui cruce custoditi, Domina casta Deipara, omnem hostis impetum facile omnes evitamus : propterea te pro debito glorificamus tanquam Matrem lucis, atque unicam spem animarum nostrarum.
O casta, quæ a Divinitatis igne nequaquam conbusta es, combure materiales affectus passionum mearum, ut cum fide, o casta, te glorificem.
Virgo Mater immaculata, virga Jesse, ex qua Christus exortus est, flos, qui vitam protulit terrigenis; per quam redempti sumus a corruptione et mortalitate; te universa, o pura, collaudamus.
In te, Virgo casta, conspectæ sunt omnes viæ Dei, qui conservavit puritatem tuam post partum manifeste sigillatam in omnia sæcula.
Ecce omnes generationes beatam te dicimus, o inviolata, magnalia tua contemplantes : tu enim supernaturaliter paris universis Conditorem Deum et hominem.


[1379] {#pg.105.1379}
Ἐν γυναιξὶ σὺ μόνη ὡραιότης, ὦ ὑπερθαύμαστε ἁγνή, ὡραιότατον τὸν Λόγον ἀποκυήσασα, τὸν ὑπὲρ πάντας φανέντα βροτοὺς ὡραῖον. Αὐτὸν οὖν ἐκδυσώπει τὸ εἶδεχθὲς καθωραῖσαι, Παρθένε, τῆς καρδίας μου.
Παιδοτόκον παρθένον τίς ἤκουσε, καὶ μητέρα πλὴν ἀνδρός; Μαρία, ἐκτελεῖς τὸ τεράστειον, ἀλλὰ φράζε μοι τὸ πῶς; Μὴ ἐρεύνα τὰ βάθη τῆς θεοτοκίας μου· τοῦτο πανάληθες, ὑπὲρ δὲ ἀνθρώπινον νοῦν ἡ κατάληψις.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς πέμπτης τῶν Βαΐων, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Τῆς ψυχῆς μου τὰ πάθη τὰ πολυώδυνα, καὶ σάρκός μου τὰς νόσους ἐν τάχει ἴασαι, πανάμωμε, καὶ τοῦ νοός μου ἐκτροπὰς στῆσον, πανάμωμε, καὶ ἐν γαλήνῃ λογισμοῦ εὐχάς; προσφέρειν καθαρὰς τῷ βασιλεῖ τῶν ἁπάντων ἀξίωσον, Θεοτόκε, καὶ ἐξαιτεῖσθαι πταισμάτων ἄφεσιν.
Ἐκ σοῦ τὸ θεῖον ὕδωρ ἀνέβλυσεν ἀθανασίας καὶ ζωῆς, Θεοκυῆτορ πανάμωμε, οὗ οἱ γευόμενοι, δίψης ἁγνή, θανατηφόρου ῥυσθήσονται.
Πύλην σε φωτὸς πεφωτισμένην βλέπει ὁ προφήτης, πανάμωμε· τὸν φωτοδότην γὰρ τίκτεις ἀφράστως ἡμῖν ὁμοιωθέντα, ὃν ὑπερυψοῦμεν εἰς πάντα τοὺς αἰῶνας.
Ἐν δυσὶ θελήσεσι καὶ οὐσίαις τέτοκας ἡμῖν Υἱὸν τὸν μονογενῆ, Κόρη, τοῦ Πατρός, γενόμενον ἄνθρωπον, ἵνα ἡμᾶς δείξῃ κοινωνοὺς φύσεως, ἄχραντε, θείας ὁ ὑπέρθεος.
Ἐκ τοῦ Κανόνος τῆς παρασκευῆς τῶν Βαΐων, εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον.
Παρθένε πανάμωμε, Μῆτερ Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ, ῥομφαία διῆλθέ σου τὴν παναγίαν ψυχήν, ἡνίκα σταυρούμενον ἔβλεψας ἑκουσίως τὸν Υἱὸν καὶ Θεόν σου, ὃν, εὐλογημένη, δυσώπουσα μὴ παύσῃ, συγχώρησιν πταισμάτων καιρῷ νηστείας δωρήσασθαι.
Ῥανίσι τοῦ ἐλέους σου κατάρδευσον, πανάχραντε, τὴν ἐκταθεῖσάν μου καρδίαν φλογμῷ τῆς ἁμαρτίας, καὶ τὸν ἐσβεσμένον μου τῆς καρδίας ἄναψον λύχνον, πύλη τοῦ φωτός.
Ἱεραί σε τῶν προφητῶν κηρύττουσι φωναὶ συμβολικῶς πύλην καὶ ὄρος, καὶ σκηνὴν τὴν ἁγίαν, φωτὸς νεφέλην, ἐξ ἧς τοῖς ἐν σκότει καὶ σκιᾷ καθημένους ἀνέτειλεν ἥλιος, Παρθένε, ὁ μόνος φωτοδότης.
Φρικτή σου ἡ λοχεία, Θεοτόκε Μῆτερ Χριστοῦ· διὸ σε μακαρίζομεν πιστῶς, καὶ δοξάζομεν σεπτῶς αἱ γενεαὶ γενεῶν εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας. Ἀμήν.
conceptionem tamen negant dicendam esse immaculatam. Nam si Virgo est immaculata, ergo nunquam inquinata est ulla macula etiam originali in conceptione, cum conceptio non sit subjectum maculæ originalis, sed sit actio nectens animam corpori, vel utriusque unio, in qua non cadit macula; sed in animam, prout substantem illi actioni vel conceptioni. Vide P. Theophilum Raynaudum in opere prænotato: Pietas Lugdunensis erga immaculatam B. Virginis Conceptionem.
Inter mulieres tu sola speciosa facta es, o casta A omni miraculo superior, quæ pulcherrimum Verbum concepisti. 321 Ipsum ergo deprecare, ut formosam reddat deformitatem cordis mei.
Puerperam virginem quis audivit, et matrem sine viro? Tu efficis hujuscemodi prodigium, Maria : sed dicito mihi, quomodo? Ne scruteris profunda divini partus mei. Verissimum est hoc; sed ab humana mente comprehendi non potest.
Ex canone in feria v, in Ramis Palmarum, ad Matutinum.
Animæ meæ passiones dolorificas et carnis meæ morbos velociter cura, o purissima : mentis meæ siste instabilitatem; et fac me dignum ut cum tranquillitate animi offeram puras preces Regi omnium, et exorem pro remissione lapsuum meorum, o Deipara.
Ex te divina scaturivit aqua immortalitatis et vitæ, o Dei Genitrix purissima et castissima; per quam, bibentes a mortifera siti liberantur.
Te lucis portam fulgidam vidit Propheta, o purissima : lucis enim datorem nobis similem factum ineffabili ratione peperisti, quem superexaltamus in universa sæcula.

and blessed, who in the flesh gave birth to God, who is superior to all praises, and who has glorified the triumphant victors over the enemy.
That He might deify human nature, God became man from you, O chaste Virgin, in an ineffable and unthinkable manner; therefore we, the faithful, with harmonious voices call you blessed.
Having been redeemed by your delivery, O blessed one, from the ancient curse, we have received a blessing, life, and redemption, we who glorify you as the Theotokos.
That God might unite with the celestial spirits those who had slipped into the great abyss of transgression, He inhabited your womb, O most inviolate one, and became flesh.
By the power of Jesus, who was incarnate of you, O Blessed Mary, you remained truly a virgin after birth, just as you had been before birth. O miracle that transcends human faith!
You appeared higher than the heavens, having given birth to the Lord and God of all, whom cease not to implore fervently and with great intensity for the salvation of those who glorify you with fervent faith.
Ezekiel sees you, O Virgin, as the closed gate, through which passed Jesus who dwelt in your womb, and took flesh from your blood without any mixture or confusion.
O most immaculate and chaste one, who gave birth to that divine light which shone forth from the Father, have mercy on my soul, darkened by the errors of life and made a mockery by the enemy, and count it worthy of the light of saving repentance, O pure one.
THEOTOKIA FROM THE PARACLETICUS
THEOTOCIA.
From the Greek Paracletica (99).
From the canon for Tuesday, at Matins. Tone I.
The orders of the incorporeal ones sing hymns to you together with us, O Virgin worthy of all praise; for you bore within your womb the One who cannot be contained.
the aid of the apostles, the way of the martyrs, the way of the confessors, and other saints, according as the nature of the day, whose office is being recited, requires. Whence it could also be called Paracleticus, as if invocatory or imploratory. It is divided into eight parts, which the Greeks call Tones. For by one tone the offices of one week are completed, by another the next, and so on for each; which being finished, they begin again from the beginning, and thus by this perpetual cycle they provide for themselves an unfailing and ever-constant material for praising God. Although the new Paracletice is also called the Octoechus, it differs from the Octoechus in that the latter only contains canons and troparia from the first Vespers to the Mass, whereas the Paracletice exhibits both that and much more, since it also demonstrates the subsequent Vespers and the entire offices of the other days during the week. It is a huge volume, but highly necessary, and one that supplies the defects of others. That it was perhaps compiled by our Joseph from the acrostic of the eighth tone Saturday is gathered by the most learned man Leo Allatius, dissert. 2, where it is held:
The divine end of the new Octoechus, but the labors of Joseph.
Furthermore, since in this volume of the Paracletice there are many canons found under the name of our Joseph, those Theotokia that exist among these [I have taken care], translated from Greek into Latin, to present to you, my reader, so that they may bear fruit and serve the Marian glory.
Theotokia from the Paracletice of the Greeks
You have given birth to him who was incarnated, remaining a virgin just as you were before birth, O spouse of God.
O holy and pure spouse of God, you holily have given birth to him who rests among the holy ones, the Son and Word coeternal with the Father, who sanctifies in the Holy Spirit those who religiously and piously sanctify him.
The Son, who formerly was begotten from the unbegotten Father, had a temporal generation, begotten from you, O Virgin, wishing to alleviate the long-standing war of the earth-born, as one who is deeply merciful, since he himself is older than all days and times.
No mouth, however eloquent and articulate, will be able to recount the ineffable miracle of your birth-giving, O spouse of God; for you give birth to the inexplicable one and carry in your arms him who holds all things by his hand.
Having conceived the fire of the fiery ministers, you appeared all-blameless, and the first-creator of all creation, O all-pure Virgin, excelling, blessed among women, filled with divine grace.

The lesser [premise] is evident: because if she had been at any time under original sin, then at that time she would have been sullied. Therefore, she would not be the Spouse "all fair" in the Virgin, nor in Christ: because he is the Spouse, and, although he is white and ruddy, and all desirable (Cant. V), yet this is not the beauty of the Spouse, but of the Spousegroom, nor in any other, because all others are accused of sin, either actual or original, and thus nowhere would the Spouse be "all fair." Thus he. See Nieremberg in the *Sacrosyllabus* § V.

If it was proper to fashion the Mother of sorrows without a stain,
It is more proper to adorn the Mother of joy.

So he wished a greater wickedness to be absent from her.
The laws of nature are made new in you; for you bring forth the Lawgiver, Christ, free from carnal laws, O altogether blameless one, legislating redemption for all who sing: The God of our fathers who is praiseworthy and super-glorious, etc.
Holy voices of the prophets long ago heralded that you would be the Mother of God, who created all things, to whom we sing, O immaculate one: Bless the Lord, etc.
You carry the One who carries all things, and you suckle with milk the One who provides food for all; great and terrifying is the mystery, surpassing your understanding, O Ark of the venerable sanctification, Virgin Mother of God: wherefore we call you blessed with faith.
From the double canon of Tuesday at Matins
As a fiery throne you carry the Creator; as a living bridal chamber and a delightful palace you contain the King, who became what we are, without change or confusion.
O most holy Lady, who alone brought forth in time the Son who is lacking in time, having become incarnate, heal all the sufferings of my most wretched soul, contracted over a long duration of time.
You bring forth the One whom the Father begot before all ages; you feed the Nourisher, having known no trial of a man. A paradoxical miracle! A new mystery, O God-graced one; wherefore every soul of the faithful glorifies you.
The Word took upon himself the whole of Adam, as a garment, proceeding from you, O Mother of God, altogether clad in flesh. Him, therefore, O Mother of God, entreat to snatch us from temptations of every kind and from the eternal fire.
The hosts of angels were struck with amazement, seeing how the Invisible One, in the form that is according to us, appeared visible from you, O Virgin Mother of God; whom, as a suppliant, entreat to deem worthy of salvation all who faithfully glorify you.
You were shown to be the sanctified temple of God who dwelt within you, O Virgin, in an ineffable manner; entreat Him that He might cleanse us from the stain of sins, so that we may be acknowledged as a house and a dwelling place of the Spirit.
You brought forth one of the venerable Trinity in two wills, bearing one substance, O free from every stain; whom earnestly entreat for the salvation of us all.
David proclaims you a Virgin Queen, O immaculate and pure; therefore I supplicate you to render me heir to the heavenly kingdom, that I may call you blessed.
Tabernacle of sanctification having been shown, O Mary, sanctify my miserable soul, defiled by the passions of concupiscent desires, and make me a participant of divine glory.
Thee, the Mother of God and fairest among women, I earnestly entreat, O Virgin; do not despise me, but have mercy on me, and keep me safe from every injury.
From all generations Christ chose you alone to be His dwelling-place, O most praiseworthy, as a venerable and pure one; and having risen from you like the sun, He illuminated the whole earth.
Offer petitions to God, O inviolate one, that He may forgive our lapses and the sins which we have committed without number, and that He may deliver us from the punishment of everlasting fire.
When the Creator of all had found you like a lily in the valleys of this world, He dwelt within you, O Mother of God; now, therefore, imbue us with the fragrances of virtue and piety.
Deliver me from foul sin and from the dark flame by which those same sins are punished, and from the gnashing of teeth, and from the worm, O Virgin, the unique protection of the human race.
With the illuminating splendors of the Word, which arose from your holy womb, O Virgin Mother of God, and which bathed the ends of the earth with light, illuminate my soul, darkened by the blackness of desires, for I have neglected to restrain my passions.
You carry Him who carries all things, like a Cherubic throne; you nourish Him who nourishes us; whom, O most dear to God, entreat without ceasing, that He may always keep us, your flock, free from the ruin of earthquakes, from hostile attack, and from every necessity.
From the canon of Wednesday, at Matins.
Wounded, O most chaste one, by the sword during the passion of your Son, as you watched Him pierced by the lance, and as He removed that sword which prevented the faithful from entering the divine paradise.
[The following section refers to the immaculate nature of the Virgin, mentioning that the membrane upon which the words of God were written required great purity, and thus the Mother of God, in whom the Word itself was written, must have been preserved free from all stain of original sin. This includes citations from Bartholomew of Pisa and Bernardino de Bustis.] it was preserved. And concerning the same in his Mariale, sermon 6, on the Conception, part 1, blessed Bernardine de Bustis wrote this: "The place must be proportioned to the location, as the Philosopher says, and the commentator in his work On the Soul and Physics." The Gloss on the chapter *Dudum* regarding tithes contributes to this, saying: "As are the predicates, such is it fitting that the subjects be." But the glorious Virgin was the habitation of the Son of God: whence she herself says in Ecclesiasticus 24: "And He who created me rested in my tabernacle." Therefore, she must have been proportioned to the inhabitant Himself, who was without any stain of sin. To a most pure Son, therefore, there could be no mother other than the most pure, nor did it befit God's Wisdom to deem a habitation defiled by sin and contaminated as fit for Himself: who, as is said in Wisdom 1, "Wisdom will not enter into a malicious soul, nor will He dwell in a body subject to sins." If, therefore, the Blessed Virgin had been defiled by original sin, she would not have been a suitable habitation for the Son of God. So Bustis.
When in the time of the passion you beheld Him who is fair in form, O Virgin, possessing neither shape nor beauty, you lamented bitterly, crying out: "Woe is me! How do You suffer, O Son, who desire to deliver all from passions?"
Beholding the unjust slaughter, O Christ, she who gave birth to You lamented and cried out to You: "O Son, Judge of justice, how are You unjustly judged, while You Yourself will to justify by righteous judgment those who had been condemned by ancient transgression and had fallen into corruption?"
Struck with amazement, the Immaculate was troubled, seeing Christ voluntarily and without complaint lifted up on the cross; and weeping, she exclaimed: "My Son and God, I who did not experience the pangs of childbirth, now endure pangs, while You are unjustly crucified by the lawless."
Beholding Your crucifixion, the most chaste one cried out: "O Son, what is this strange sight that I see? You, who heal the passions of the infirm, how do You endure a passion entirely new and unusual? What have Your enemies rendered unto You, O Benefactor, for the benefits which they received from You?"
He whom you brought forth, O Virgin, in a wondrous manner brought forth the ages, and being lifted up on the cross, He lifted up with Himself those who had fallen, and showed them to be citizens of heaven, crying: "Blessed are You, the God of our fathers."
O Maiden, who have not known wedlock, with whom all creation glorifies you, seeing you, Jesus, Redeemer of all, long ago lifted up on the cross, she cried out: "That foolish people, who provoked You to anger, took You to lift You from the earth. I am left childless, and I am shaken, and as a mother, I am pained in my heart."
Having in an ineffable manner brought forth the Word who loves mankind, when you saw Him suffering voluntarily for men, O Maiden, you cried out: "What is this? God, who is impassible, undergoes a passion, that He might deliver from passions those who faithfully worship Him."
*From the canon for Thursday, at Matins.*
Remove the obscurities of my mind, O most pure one, with your light, and snatch me from eternal darkness, that I may perpetually sing your magnalia.
Deliver me, O most clean one, from every adversity, and from the many scandals of the serpent, and from the eternal fire and darkness, you who brought forth for men the light that never sets.
The prophet loved by the Holy Spirit long ago described you, O chaste Mother of God, as a shady mountain, you who now refresh with the shade of grace those who have been melted by the flame of many sins, through your most acceptable intercessions.
O Maiden, most full of the grace of God, most resplendent temple of Christ, by your intercessions make us, through our good works, dwellings of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit.
Christ performs magnalia for you, O Virgin...
and that He may always magnify in me His rich mercies, O you who are full of grace.
O most holy Virgin, Mother of God, we your servants, always by night and by day, with a contrite heart, prayerfully entreat you, that you may benignly grant us the remission of our many sins, O gracious one.
Heal, I pray, the passions of my heart, you who have brought forth the Physician of all, and make me a partaker of the portion of the righteous, O Virgin, by appeasing Christ through your prayers.
O most immaculate one, who gave birth to the divine Light, illuminate me who am enveloped in darkness due to the manifold snares of the enemy, while I lead my life in extreme sloth and provoke God to wrath; and lead me by the hand to honest and holy works, as you are the cause of all good things.
From the Canon of Friday, at Matins.

By the splendor of Him who was born of you in the flesh, O Virgin, enlighten my mind and illuminate my heart, dispelling the darkness of sin and banishing all the mist of my sloth.

Me, always falling from my resolve, unstable in mind, led away and enticed by wicked thoughts, for having, in my extreme madness, given myself over entirely to the enemies that they might take me most easily, do not despise, O Lady.

O Virgin all-luminous, who brought forth God, the Giver of light, enlighten the eyes of my heart with the light of divine knowledge, that I may sing: "All you works, praise the Lord and exalt Him throughout all ages."
The intellectual hosts tremble, seeing the divine radiance of the Father held ineffably in your arms, O Virgin Mother most pure, possessing the likeness and image of you, that He might make men like unto God.

O unique girl, most dear to God, who contained the fountain of in-passibility potentially—that her immunity from the same original sin be denoted herein is necessary. wounded by passions, O Maiden, offer your healing hand, and rescue me from eternal fire, O only divinely-graced one. The Creator of times is believed to have received from you, O Mother and Ever-Virgin, a beginning under time.

From the canon for Tuesday at Matins
O flame-bearing tongs, which Isaiah once saw, burn up the material passions of my heart, O Theotokos, and utterly vanish and destroy them.
You nourish like a mother the Nourisher of all, and you carry Him in your arms, O chaste one, Him who always carries and sustains all things in His hand.
Mindful in the Spirit of the prophets, O Theotokos, the glorious choir, taught by the Spirit, described in many ways by sacred symbols this mystery of yours which is beyond understanding, the fulfillment of which we clearly behold.
Daniel saw you as a great mountain in the Spirit, O Theotokos, from which a stone was cut and shattered the graven images of the demons.

That He might deify us, God was incarnate from your most pure blood, and was made mortal, O Virgin Theotokos; whom perpetually entreat on behalf of those who honor you.
With the most radiant beams of that Sun which rose for us from your womb, and which has banished the night of godlessness, O Virgin-Mother Mary, enlighten all who honor you with faith, and deliver them from the dark gloom in the hour of judgment.
From the other canon of the third day, to the holy Forerunner.
You were a kinsman, O blessed Forerunner, of that chaste Virgin from whom God received bodily form; with whom we also venerate you, and dwelling now within your divine temple, we pray that you may make us houses of the Holy Spirit.

Look upon me in my sickness, O all-pure one, and loosen my grave and incurable passions, so that I may magnify you, who have magnified the whole human race.

sterile womb carried you, O Virgin, who in your womb carried the Word made flesh; whom the great Forerunner, the most holy fruit of a sterile womb, recognized with divine leapings, rejoicing, and worshiped.

You, O Prophet, worshiped Him who carries all things by His nod, when He was carried in the womb of the Theotokos; with her, pray for the salvation of my wretched soul, which falls daily into many sins.
From the Canon of the Fourth [Day] for Matins.
The holy choir called you, O most holy Lady, an impassable gate, an elect earth, and a mountain that the hand of the stonecutter did not touch; for you brought forth the Lord of all, who voluntarily underwent crucifixion in the flesh.
The most pure one, when she beheld the Giver of life exalted on the wood, and the same voluntarily dying, and providing life to all, felt her bowels racked with inward grief.
The splendor of the Father, Christ, has arisen from your womb, O maiden ignorant of the marital bed; and by being crucified, He illuminated the whole circle of the earth, and dispelled the darkness of the demons.
You were found incorrupt after childbirth, O chaste one; for God was born from you as man; whom, beholding hanging on the cross, you could not endure the pain of your bowels, and you broke forth in plaintive cries.
We praise you, O most worthy of all praise, in that you brought forth God, who is above all praise, who by His power destroyed the tyrant, the prince of evils, on the wood, for our salvation and glory.
The ripened cluster, which you brought forth without the cultivation of man, O chaste one, as you saw it hanging on the wood, you uttered these voices: O my sweetest Son, distill that must, through which the drunkenness of the passions may be prohibited.
Your Son, O Lady most bright before God, willingly took flesh from your womb; and when He was invisible, He became visible, and underwent the torment of the cross, and was named a curse, while He delivers all from the curse.
You renovated the corrupted nature of the first parent, by bringing forth in a supernatural manner the Creator of universal nature, [original-script column: see facsimile] [in the Regestum, page 563 and following.]
A ...and, remaining a virgin undefiled after the birth, you saw Him whom you had once seen hanging upon the cross, o most holy Virgin Mother, and you broke forth into weeping and groans.
*From the Canon for Friday at Matins.*
The word of the venerable prophet is fulfilled: for a sword has pierced through your own soul, O Lady, when you beheld your Son wounded by a lance upon the wood. The ranks of the incorporeal beings honor you, for you gave birth to the Lord of all clothed in a body, O Maiden, Bride of God; He who by the wood loosed all who were bound, and bound the faithful together with His love. The Most High is incarnate from your chaste blood, O most pure one; and when you beheld Him unjustly lifted up upon the wood, you sighed with tears and magnified His compassion. He who is borne in the heavens upon the shoulders of the Cherubim, as is fitting for God, was without doubt carried in your arms; and being fixed to the cross, He saved all from corruption. In order that He might deify man, God is born of you, O Virgin, and is crucified, and tastes death, slaying him who had formerly slain me. You have become the living palace of the King and a fiery throne, O Virgin, in which He sitting raised all men from the primal fall, and honored them with a seat with the Father. The spotless ewe-lamb, the beauty of both prophets and martyrs, when she saw you as a Lamb suspended upon wood, began to weep bitterly and to say: O all you creatures that exist in the nature of things, praise the Lord and supremely exalt Him throughout all ages. The beauty of the luminaries departed from its accustomed course when it saw you, O Sun of righteousness, voluntarily lifted up upon the cross. But the Virgin, with the virgin disciple, mingled bitter lamentations with her words, crying out: What is this new and strange sight?
*From the Canon for Saturday at Matins.*
The venerable women who had struggled with hard discipline, and had athleticly overcome the enemy, standing around you, O Mother of God, rejoiced. Together with all the prophets and holy women, O Virgin, earnestly entreat God who was born of you to have mercy on us. The prophet Habakkuk saw you, O Mother of God, as a mountain shadowed by virtues, from which God appeared in an inexplicable way, who by His virtue covered the heavens and saved mankind from destruction. O most holy Virgin, glory of martyrs, confessors, and the rest of the just, deliver us from every tyranny of temptations.
O Holy Theotokos, sanctify our thoughts and strengthen our understanding, and save us from the arrows of the wicked, keeping us unharmed as we glorify thy mercy, O pure one. Together with the martyrs, O immaculate one, and the holy Fathers, and the prophets, and all the holy women, entreat Him who rests alone in the saints to sanctify us who glorify Him with holy voices unto the ages.
O pure Mother of God, with all the martyrs, prophets, and holy ones, and holy women, and hieromartyrs, entreat the Savior to have mercy upon us. O Mother, most pure Virgin, who in the flesh didst give birth to God, the lover of mercy, ever entreat Him, together with all the saints, that He may keep us safe from dangers.

From the canon for the second day at Matins

Grant me, O pure one, true repentance; still the swells of my passions, O protection of those who are sinning.
Thou who alone on earth didst give birth to the Word through the Word, and remainedst a virgin, deliver me from brutal actions so that I may praise thee with words full of reverence.
We praise the Ark of the Testament, the table that is truly holy, our propitiation, the living temple of God, the all-golden lampstand, the true Mother of the Creator.
O fountain that pourest forth the water of remission, dry up the flowing waters of my transgressions, granting me showers of tears, that I may ever praise thee as Theotokos.
By an excess of profligacy, I have now drawn near to Hades; O Maiden, since thou hast exceeding compassion, as thou didst ineffably give birth to the most compassionate God, have mercy on me and save me.
O luminous palace of the Master, show me to be a house of light; O impenetrable gate, open for me the paths of repentance; O holy land, lead me to the land of the meek; O Lady, show me to be free from all dominion of the passions.
From the canon for the third day at Matins

O immaculate Mother-Virgin, thou who art Lady of all, grant to us all who are led by repentance to Christ…
that we may find him propitious.
Hail, O Maiden conspicuous by the appellation of many names, from whom the Word was born, who delivers us from the irrationality of foolish actions. Hail, O luminous cloud, who dispellest the clouds of our pusillanimity and sloth.
By thy birth-giving, O Maiden, thou hast taken away the curse of the first parent, and hast caused to flow for us streams of blessing, for we bless thee and with faith glorify thee.
O gate of those who through faith attain salvation, O portal through which alone he passed who for our sake was incarnate, open to us the gates of justice, who faithfully praise thee.
O chaste and perpetual Virgin, deliver me from the darkness of ignorance and sin, which everywhere surrounds and constrains me; since thou hast brought forth him who contains and binds the universe.
The Lord, beautiful in form, when he had loved the beauty of Jacob, O most immaculate one, dwelt in thy womb, and adorned the human race with the beauty of heavenly gifts.
The prophet foresaw thee as a luminous cloud, from which the great Sun, Christ our God, appeared to us and illuminated those things that were formerly steeped in darkness. Beseech him, therefore, O most kind one, to dispel the clouds of my passions and to illumine me with divine light.
From the canon for the Fourth Day at Matins.
Thou hast appeared more sublime than the heavenly orders, having brought forth on earth the Word of God, who, O immaculate one, through his sufferings and his precious cross, in his goodness, led back and received us into the heavenly kingdoms.
When thou didst see Christ suspended on the cross, O unwedded one, thou didst lament and say: O Sun of glory, thou art perishing from my eyes, yet thou enlightenest those who dwell in darkness.
The prophet once beheld thee, O immaculate Maiden, as a scroll in which, by the finger of the Father, the Word was inscribed as he became incarnate; and by his lance he rent the handwriting of the first parent, O immaculate one.
Our nature, which had previously flowed away on account of transgression, thou didst reshape, having without loss brought forth Christ, O most holy Lady, shield of our souls.
When thou didst see the death of the Word who was incarnate from thy blood, O most immaculate Lady and Virgin Mother, thou didst cry out with motherly affection, and didst magnify him who was the author of life.
Thou didst stand beholding Christ fixed to the cross, whom thou hadst brought forth, O immaculate one, and thou didst cry out: Do not leave me without child, whom thou didst preserve a Virgin even after birth-giving.
When I am to be judged before the tribunal of Christ, as one who is guilty, O pure one, show thyself to me, that thou mayest deliver me from every torment by thy fervent protection.
In time thou didst bring forth him who is more ancient than all time, who loosens the bonds of time with his own bonds, O immaculate one, the first-formed Adam
from the bonds of his sweet love.
Bearing human form, thou hast brought forth the Radiance of the Father, O Virgin, whom the sun could not endure to behold when he was lifted up upon the Cross, and was darkened, and was diminished by the gloom of idolatry; therefore, together with him, we magnify thee.
From the Canon of Thursday at Matins
The divine ark, which contained the Giver of the Law, who takes away all our iniquities through the bowels of his divine mercy, an ineffable sea, Mary, immaculate in all things, let us all with one voice magnify.
Reason cannot conceive the production beyond reason of thy wonderful Conception, O Mother of God, Virgin Mother, therefore, I beseech thee, grant grace to my mind, that I may ever glorify thee.
Thou didst bring forth the Word, having been made flesh through an excess of compassion, unspeakably; entreat him, O immaculate one, to deliver all from carnal defilements and from all the hardships of life.
As the only joy of the angels, fill me, O beloved of God, with thy joy, for my mind is saddened and sunken in negligence.
The Word, having dwelt in thy womb, O Mother chaste, cast out the corruption which had long dwelt in mankind because of the transgression, as only God, through the bowels of his mercy.
Illumine me, whose soul is darkened by negligence, O chaste one, rekindling the lamp of my heart, that with goodly zeal I may glorify thee.
Power and praise is he who was born of thee, O all-blameless one, whom do thou earnestly entreat, O Virgin, that he may strengthen me who am weakened by the passions, to fulfill his saving ordinances.
Be my saving illumination, thou who brought forth the Light, chasing away the many and grievous clouds of my soul, that I may be born a son of the day through thy prayers, doing holy works, and that I may always bless thee with hymns.
From the Canon of Friday at Matins
The most beautiful and Virgin Lady, when she beheld [the One] whom she brought forth
voluntarily exalted upon the tree, cried out, lamenting with sorrow: "O most merciful Lord God of all, being Lord of glory, how dost thou suffer these things, O Master?"
Thou wast preserved a virgin even after childbirth, O chaste one, who didst bring forth the incarnate God, who was fastened to the cross by his own will. As tongs, thou didst receive the divine coal, O Virgin Mother, which did not consume thee in the least, but rather bedewed thy incorrupt and divine womb; beholding Him exalted upon the tree in the flesh by his own will, thou didst glorify him with hymns.
When thou didst behold, O chaste Virgin, the One who of old formed Eve from the rib of the side, fastened to the wood of the cross and pierced in the side by a lance, thou wast moved with maternal sorrow and cried out: "How dost thou die, my Son, who art the immortal life?"
The mind of men is not able to grasp the miracle of thy childbirth, O Maiden most worthy of all praise: for thou broughtest forth beyond all capacity of the mind. Thou alone, O chaste one, didst have an ineffable conception and an unutterable childbirth, having clothed with a body the Lord who was crucified in the flesh, whom praising we cry out: "Blessed art thou, O God of our fathers," etc.
Thee, a golden urn and a candelabrum, a table and a rod, a divine mountain and a cloud, the palace of the King and a throne of fire, O Theotokos, do all the faithful name, thou who wast kept a virgin after childbirth.
The rays of the sun, beholding thy crucifixion, O my Son, were darkened; and how did the obstinate people of the Hebrews not darken, delivering thee to death, the Author of life? With such words the Deipara used to speak; whom we magnify without ceasing.
From the Canon of Saturday at Matins.
Thee, O most chaste one, who brought forth God in the flesh, we honor with pious mind without ceasing, together with the venerable women who, having been enlightened by exercise in the struggle, cast down the enemy.
We who have been saved, O most pure one, by thy venerable childbirth, cry out faithfully to thee with the voice of Gabriel, "Hail" (18); and we beseech thee, that by thy intercessions thou mayest obtain for us all the forgiveness of sins.
Let us worthily praise the Mother of God, the glory of the martyrs, the divine ornament of the hierarchs and of the holy confessors, the proclamation of the prophets, and the pride of women. From thee, O pure one, God has been incarnate, and has now revealed thee to be higher than the angels, and excelling all created things; wherefore we hymn thee. Thou art the glory of the martyrs, the prophets, and of all the holy righteous from all ages, O pure one; whence we honor thee clearly with sacred voices together with them unto the ages. Thou art truly the glory of the martyrs and confessors, of the prophets, of women, and of all the hierarchs who have lived in holiness, O most pure one, with whom we pay thee honor. Let the most lauded Virgin, the resounding proclamation of the prophets, the ornament of the hierarchs and the athletes and the holy ones, and the joy of holy women, be hymned with obligation unto the ages. "Terrible is this place," cried Jacob with a loud voice when he saw thy type in the ladder, O Mother of God, truthfully; the glory of the martyrs, the pride of the holy ones, the splendor of the angels, the salvation of all the prophets and the faithful.
TONUS IV
Ex canone feria II ad Matutinum.
O most radiant lamp of the Sun of glory, kindle the flame of my soul, which is always extinguished by sloth and torpor, and cherish it with the oil of divine works, O most holy one, that I may glorify thee with faith and affection. O Maria Lady, who hast given birth to the Lord of all; deliver and illuminate my mind, which is oppressed under the dominion of perverse affections and blackened by malice. As the abyss of wisdom, Jesus came down upon thee, having found thee alone to be pure, O God-bearing Virgin, and He dried up the swirling torrents of sin with the flames of divine grace. The prophetic voices have set forth thy wonders, O inviolate one, calling thee a gate and a shining lamp, O chaste one: from which the truly admirable radiance illuminates the whole world. Illuminate my heart, darkened by the shadowy assaults of sin, O Bride of God, with thy light; for thou hast given birth to the Sun himself. Thou alone remainest after childbirth, O Lady, gleaming with virginal beauty, alone having escaped the lot of mothers; for thou alone didst give birth to God, the Redeemer of our souls.
A (19) [Note: Friar Johannes Bromyard, in his *Summa praedicantium*, part II, chapter 3, while stating other things, says: "The Son of God, wishing in some place and from some woman to assume our nature and to dwell with us, found from the beginning of the world none worthy for a lodging, because all were infected by original or actual sin, or both. And at length, however, at the end of the world finding this most clean Virgin, in whom there was no sin, neither original nor actual, as if rejoicing at the desired good fortune found, he was able to say: 'This is my rest,' etc."]
OF SAINT JOSEPH THE HYMNOGRAPHER
All creatures bless thy Son, who has crowned us with blessings and taken away the curse; O thou who alone art heaped with every blessing, glorious Virgin, who hast filled our race with grace.
By the rays of the light that is in thee, O Bride of God, illuminate my soul, raise me up as I lie in the pit of destruction, routing the enemies who constantly afflict my heart and urge me on toward the passions.
From the double canon of the third day at Matins.
Let us honor with hymns the Virgin Mary, the immaculate Tabernacle, the Ark, the Table, and the Mountain from which the stone—Christ, the Lord of all—was hewn without human hands.
O bright cloud, dissolve the many and grievous clouds of my soul through thy illuminative intercessions, so that I may see the rays of Him who rose from thee, and may receive an unsetting light through the light.
Thou hast taken away the sadness of our first parents, having brought forth for us the joy, the Giver of life and Redeemer, O all-holy Theotokos, whom thou must entreat with intense affection for the salvation of our souls.
Through thee the world has found mercy, which had been rendered useless by transgression, O Mother-Virgin; therefore, with songs of praise it rightly calls thee blessed.
I call upon thee for salvation at every time and in every place; do not despise me, O all-blameless one, who hast brought forth God, the Redeemer and my Savior together.
propitiation and redemption for us has appeared, thy Son, O Virgin, whom do thou entreat to save those who with contrition of soul call thee blessed.
(20) Being thus also heaped with this blessing, because she was the dawn of the divine Sun, unobscured by any original darkness, an Ark always crowned, and in no moment of time subject to the demon through sin; a most splendid whiteness, never found in any, even the slightest, obscurity of sin, and the friend of God, all beautiful, from whom every stain, not only actual but also of original sin, always fled. Furthermore, Christ (as Bartholomaeus Pisanus says in his work *De laudibus. Virginis*, lib. 1, fruct. 7) gave greater gifts and fewer necessary ones to the Virgin. It is clear that since He made her His own Mother, He therefore gave the lesser gifts, namely, preservation from original sin. Likewise, He preserved the Virgin from the punishment of Eve, therefore also from the guilt, etc.
MARIALE
Make to arise for me, O Lady Virgin, a ray of your mercy, for me who lie in the darkness of my sins; and lead me to the light of repentance, O inviolate one, that I may praise you with faith.
The God of Jacob loved your beauty, O Virgin Maid, adorning through you all those who had previously been darkened by transgression.
Most holy Virgin, the protection of all the faithful, stand by; rescue me from the threats and darkness of the fearful judgment in the hour of trial, that I may always praise you with faith.
The images of the Law clearly set forth your venerable childbirth, O Spouse of God; and we, now beholding their fulfillment, O Lady, rightfully glorify you.
O Mother in the flesh of the Savior and God, and Redeemer, and Lord, O most pure Lady, continually entreat Him for us, O immaculate one, that having received remission for our many terrible sins, we may celebrate with hymns His mercies which no mind can comprehend.
Keep your servants, O Virgin, from evil capture, and from spoliation, and from the slavery of the most cruel enemy, those who always glorify you with heart and tongue.
Your womb, O most immaculate one, was shown to the world as a heap of the venerable threshing floor, bearing the Bread of Life that nourishes all things; wherefore we, the faithful, with one voice hymn you as the cause of all good things.
As the fountain of our reformation, reform and restore me, who am utterly crushed by the strikes of the serpent, that with faith and longing I may call you blessed, O Theotokos, Virgin worthy of all hymns.
O Maid who loves what is good, having given birth to the good God, make me good—I cry out to you—my soul wretchedly defiled and miserable with passions and the wicked traps of the deceiver, that I may praise you with faith, the hope of all.
O lover of good things who gave birth to the good God, O good Mother of God, beseech Him to rescue me from every evil, and...
(25) The most pious and learned man, Leonard of Nogarolis, in his dedicatory epistle of his Office of the Immaculate Conception to Pope Sixtus IV, writes, among other things: "No one who bursts forth into praises of Mary would say that she, who is the Mother of God, was at the same time once a daughter of the devil. You truly render the one you praise deformed. Will you call her the Mother of grace, who was once an enemy to the Giver of grace? It is the task of an ungrateful person, not one who praises. Will you affirm a way to life in one who herself once did not know the way? You surely confuse yourself. Is she the hope of sinners, and the medium for the removal of all sins, who had tasted sin? You show yourself to be a stranger to the truth. You exalt and you de- press, you magnify and you vilify, you heal and you strike; you ensnare yourself in the censure of your own words, calling blessed the one whom you do not cease to curse. Do you wish, therefore, to praise the Blessed Virgin as she enters the world? Do you wish to establish a feast day? Attribute to her those praises which do not serve to bring disgrace." Certainly, it is doubtful to no one that to be conceived without original sin is a great praise; therefore, it is not to be denied to the Virgin, to whom the Church does not seem to have denied it when it calls her holy in her conception. It is one thing to be worthy of praise, and another to be most worthy. One who is worthy of praise is sufficiently extolled by some praises offered to her; but one who is most worthy of praise deserves to be praised in all things that are not repugnant to her. Since Mary, therefore, is most worthy of praise, she is to be extolled in all things and above all, and consequently also in her conception. Moreover, the Emperor Emmanuel Palaiologos used to say that this is something that necessarily happens to all who wish to have any discourse about Mary; for it will be one of two things: either to say nothing at all about the Virgin, or, if you speak, to praise her. For it is impossible in any way for you to speak of her and for what you say not to be praise; since both she herself, and whatever pertains to her, possesses most celebrated glory. Thus he.
A ...malice, and that my heart may be wounded by His love, with hatred for the voluptuous passions of the flesh, so that I may be able to celebrate You, the most praiseworthy one, with hymns.
From the canon for Wednesday at Matins
After childbirth, Virgin, you remained inviolate, having ineffably brought forth Him who was exalted upon the cross; wherefore we all, the faithful, O immaculate one, proclaim you blessed with loud voices.
The immaculate ewe-lamb, seeing You, O Lord, lifted up on the cross, was weeping painfully, O Master, while praising Your power.
You who within time brought forth the Lord who is beyond time, and who alone possessed immaculate virginity; seeing the same One suspended upon the wood, you felt your soul being sliced and divided by pains.
He who alone is good, when He had entered into the immaculate womb to take on flesh, even when crucified wished to be shown, that He might deliver us from perdition.
The iniquitous people fastened You with nails, and now the sword of pain rends my heart, O my Creator. Thus the Virgin cried out with tears.
Seeing Christ, the God who appeared as dew in the furnace and in no way burned your womb, hanging upon the wood, O Maiden, you glorified His incomprehensible condescension.
Seeing Christ dying, and at the same time destroying that enemy who was killing mankind, the most praised Lady praised Him as Master with tears; and admiring His long-suffering and patience, she cried out: "All you works, praise the Lord." B 
Illuminate, chaste Virgin, my soul shrouded in the darkness of sin; dissolve the clouds of my evils, O cloud of light, who once saw the sun darkened while He who is immortal was being crucified.
From the canon for Thursday at Matins
Virgin, possessing unceasing intercession on our behalf, put to sleep the passions of our soul by your sacred entreaties, granting unto us divine and salvific wakefulness for the fulfillment of God’s will.
We possess you as a protection, most holy Virgin; change our sadness into joy, and deliver us from the tribulation that brings forth death. C 
The hosts of the angels are struck with astonishment, O most chaste one, singing hymns to the greatness of your divine childbirth; with whom, Virgin, pour out prayers for the salvation of all those who with sincere faith call you blessed.
Looking upon you with intellectual eyes, O Virgin, Isaiah erupted into these words: "Behold, Jesus the Lord is about to be born, that men may be reborn." D 
Your people and city suppliantly pray to you, Mother of God. Deliver us from all necessity, O most holy Lady, and from the eternal condemnation of the coming age.
Put to sleep the rising stirrings of the passions of my soul
with your sleepless intercession; grant me wakefulness, O Maiden, driving far away the drowsiness of sloth.
Make me worthy of your compassion, you who gave birth to the Word endowed with supreme compassion, O blessed, most chaste Virgin; and at the hour of judgment, stand by me, that you may rescue me from the sentence of condemnation there, O pure one.
We bring to you the voice of the divine Gabriel with joy, and we cry aloud: Hail, Paradise, who always have the tree of life in your midst; O most glorious palace of the Word, Hail, Virgin worthy of all praise.
From the Canon for Friday at Matins
sword, as Simeon said, pierced your heart when you saw Christ crucified alone, O Lady, being pierced by the lance; whence, lamenting, you endured sorrows.
From you the restoration of Eve was truly seen, O Virgin Lady most dear to God: for God was born in the flesh, and on the cross was exalted, and cast down the demons, O Lady full of grace.
The handwriting of Adam is torn up, while you are pierced by the lance, O Master, the Theotokos cried out, standing by the cross, Lord, and wailing in pain.
When you saw Christ, whom you bore in the flesh, pinned to the wood, O you who are full of divine grace, you were filled with lamentations, and you truly marveled at his long-suffering; whence you magnified his condescension.
Be a harbor for me, O all-blameless one, for I am sailing upon a sea of troubles; you who saved the whole of human nature, which had suffered shipwreck, by your giving birth, O God-bearing Maiden, now save me.
O spotless Bride, palace of the Creator, untilled earth, you have been shown to be a fiery-formed throne, O all-blameless one; whence we cry aloud to you: Hail, Lady, who have deified mortals by your divine conception.
When the all-blameless Mother saw him sleeping upon the wood, who provides divine and saving wakefulness to all: wailing with pain, she cried out: What is this most strange miracle? He who gives life to all, dies willingly?
Illumine, O chaste one, those who with affection praise and magnify you; dissolve the darkness of our passions, O Maiden; calm the tempest; put away the scandals of the wicked; subjugate the sons of Hagar to the most pious emperor by your intercessions.
From the Canon for Saturday at Matins
The Son of God, desiring to free us from the ancient condemnation, was pleased, O pure one, to become your Son.
Wherefore, being adopted as sons through him, we bless the heavenly Father, singing hymns to you. Let us offer praise to God, who of his own will was born of the Virgin, and joined the choirs of women to the choirs of angels. He who kept you a Virgin even after birth has glorified all the virgins who surround you; with them, plead without ceasing that our souls may be preserved from all distress and temptation. The Lord, having dwelt in you, O all-immaculate one, made men the dwellings of his divine glory, O Virgin. With mouth and tongue and heart, I confess you, O Maiden, to be the pure Mother of our God; but by your mediation, deliver me from the sentence of eternal condemnation. Seeing of old the flame not burning the bush, the lawgiver learned that this was a figure of your child-bearing, O Mother of God, alone ever-blessed. Make me worthy of your compassion, you who gave birth to the most compassionate Word, O all-pure Mother of God, and save me as I cry: All you works, bless, sing hymns to the Lord. You have appeared as higher than the heavens, O all-blameless one, for you have carried the One who contains all things; make my mind also superior to the passions of the flesh, granting me strength whereby I may be able to perform the will of the Master. PLAGAL TONE 1. From the Canon for Tuesday at Matins. O gate of divine glory, open to me the gates of repentance, and rescue my humble soul, I pray, from the gates of hell. O Virgin Mother, who gave a body to God, receive the voices of those who cry to you at all times, and deliver us from various adversities. The Lord is incarnate from your pure blood, O you who must be celebrated with all hymns, granting through your most kindly intercession a place of repentance to those who honor you, as the most compassionate and only lover of mankind. O Virgin, blessed among women, bestow your mercies upon your people, for you have been shown to be the Mother of the merciful.
O Virgin, the defense of the faithful, beseech the Lord to deliver your servants from all sin.
Blessed are you, O God, who entered the womb of the Virgin and brought salvation to men; you are to be praised above all, and surpassingly glorious.
From you, O God, did dawn, and the knowledge of God illumined those who were in darkness, O Virgin praised by all.
You bear, as a fiery throne, with a nod the One who bears all things, and you nurse with milk, O Virgin, Him who provides nourishment to all.
From the double canon of the third day at Matins
Release me, O chaste Virgin, from evil habit; establish me upon the rock of the divine commandments, O Lady, for I am being whirled about and turned around by the snares of the ancient supplanter; and make me worthy to please Christ by singing well and chanting a song of victory.
You gave birth ineffably, without travail, to Him whom the Father begot before the ages without change; entreat Him, O Lady most praised, that those who run to you may be preserved from every injury.
Open to me now the doors of repentance, O Gate of light, O Virgin, and close the entrances of the passions to my humble soul.
Holy Virgin-Mother, alone praised by all, deliver us from the stain of offenses, enlighten our understanding, sanctify our hearts, and deliver us all from eternal condemnation, we pray.
The all-pure Lady, the defense of those who sin, the divine restoration of those who fall—as she gave birth to God, she is glorified.
Have mercy on me, alone praised by all, for you gave birth in an ineffable manner to the merciful God because of the extreme greatness of His goodness, and deliver me from eternal punishment.
O Gate of glory, unapproachable by any, O undefiled one, open to me the doors of repentance, providing for me the divine entrances and the rest that is there.
In an ineffable Word, O Virgin-Mother, you gave birth to God, who gives repentance to us all who have committed many sins, through your good intercession, O refuge and harbor of the faithful.
O tabernacle most dear to God, venerable ark of sanctification, lampstand of divine light, table of the bread of life, living palace of the Word, make me a temple of the Holy Spirit.
I am tossed by the storm of sin, O undefiled and chaste one; hasten to rescue me, leading me to the harbors of repentance, O all-blameless one.
You have been shown to be a saving harbor to all, O chaste one, assuaging the surge of the passions, and leading all to the serenity of repentance, O venerable one—even those who are lowly upon the earth.
You have brought forth a new child, O most pure one, Christ, who refashions us who had grown old through the ancient transgression into a new creation. Glory of angels, salvation of men, be in the place of a surety for me, O Mother of God, that I may turn and repent, and receive forgiveness for sins committed in knowledge and ignorance. You have raised us from the abyss of the fall by your exalted birth, O you who are full of divine grace; wherefore, O Maiden, we praise you with voices of thanksgiving in faith, unto all ages. A light-bearing cloud that went before the new people to the land of promise, you have truly been shown, O you who are full of divine grace, and a gate leading to life; therefore, O Theotokos, we magnify you. You were truly shown to be the light-bearing carriage of that Sun who shone from your womb, O immaculate and chaste one, and who dissolved the grievous darkness of deceit; wherefore in faith we rightly magnify you.
From the canon for the fourth day at Matins
When you beheld the Lord, born from your womb, being unjustly exalted upon the wood of the cross, O Virgin, you shed tears, and you truly praised his infinite condescension. Angelic choirs praise you, O Maiden worthy of all praise, for you surpass them incomparably in dignity; for you gave birth in the flesh to God, who by the wood effaced the curse that came by the wood, and caused the fountain of blessing to flow. A sword, O blessed Mother of God, pierced your soul when you saw your Son being crucified and commending his soul into the hands of the Father. We call you the most immaculate Mother of God, through whom the curse was withered away and extinguished, and redemption and blessing were given. Your offspring is wonderful, O Mother of God, who performs great wonders and is glorified in the saints; you are the only completely wonderful Lady. Your conception is strange, O most immaculate one; for you gave birth to God, who extinguished the flame of malice by the wood and illumined the world. You stood by the cross, contemplating, O Maiden, O Lady, Christ whom you had borne, as he was fixed upon it; wherefore you cried out: Leave me not destitute of offspring, O eternal Son and Word of the eternal Father. You brought forth the planter of religion and piety, the Creator himself, who planted true knowledge upon the earth and dissolved the curse that had sprouted from the plant, and while we magnify him, we also call you, O Virgin, blessed.
From the Canon for Thursday, at Matins.
Of old, the chorus of prophets called thee, O Virgin, a divine mountain and a gate impassable; wherefore we beseech thee, O Mother-Virgin, open unto us the gates of repentance.
Thou didst stand at the right hand of Christ as a Queen, O Maiden overflowing with divine grace, truly clad in golden fringes, procuring for us the kingdom of heaven by thy mediation and intercessions.
O holy Theotokos, help me who am immersed continually in the pleasures of the body, and who am always lying and groaning on the bed of sloth.
He whom no thought can comprehend, O Maiden overflowing with divine grace, having become circumscribed by taking on a body, enclosed Himself within thee, that He might deliver men from the straits by which they were held. B 
O Mother of God, having conceived without the work of a man, thou didst appear a chaste Virgin; therefore I entreat thee with faith, banish the sloth of my soul.
From thee has shone forth for us the sun that knows no setting, the light-bearing cloud, Christ our God, illuminating those who were held in the darkness of ignorance, O Theotokos.
God, having assumed flesh from thee for our sake, O chaste one, reveals thee as the divine protection, most immaculate, for all our race; wherefore we, the faithful, praise thee with a loud voice.
The prophet saw thee, O chaste one, as a radiant lampstand bearing the intelligible light, Christ, through whom we who were lying in the darkness of evils have been illuminated, and we call thee blessed, O ever-virgin Theotokos.
From the Canon for Friday, at Matins.
