Thou didst bring forth for us, O chaste Virgin, a new infant, the Ancient of Days, who by His divine passion renewed the nature of men that had grown old, O most immaculate one.
The author of life for us, dying on the wood, has been revealed as thy Son and Lord, O chaste one, who glorifies those who praise thee.
As thou didst see on the cross Christ whom thou didst bring forth, O chaste one, thou didst marvel at His ineffable long-suffering; wherefore we glorify thee together with Him.
Seeing the Master fallen asleep upon the wood, thou didst cry out lamenting, O most immaculate Mother-Virgin. D 
A sword pierced through thy heart, O most immaculate Virgin, when thou didst behold the Creator being crucified and His divine side being pierced by a lance.
after childbirth a Virgin, just as you were before childbirth, and so you remained. For it was God who was being born, that he might make men gods.
Gabriel, the matchmaker and groomsman of the divine nuptials, having been sent to you, O Virgin, shouted out saying: Hail, most luminous palace of Christ, King of all, in whom, having dwelt, he shall deify all men.
light has risen for us from you, O pure one, Jesus, who, being crucified, bathed his creatures in joyful light and banished the darkness of the underworld.
From the Canon of the Sabbath, at Matins.
To you, worthy of all praises, we cry out: Rejoice, you who have given birth to Joy itself. Illuminate our mind and soul, and lead all to the paths of knowledge; and beseech your Son and God, O spotless Lady, that he may grant to us all the forgiveness of sins.
As the company of women saw you, Blessed among women, it strove to offer itself to your Son, O Virgin Mother.
He who was eternal has now been made subject to time from you, O Virgin; entreat him now, O Virgin, that he may blot out all the sins of my soul committed over a long time.
We praise you, O Virgin, through whom God appeared on earth, having become man. Hail, most fertile earth, which brought forth the mystical ear of corn that nourishes every breath.
You, O Lord, who at the beginning formed Eve; having entered the Virgin’s womb, you work my reformation, having put on the form of a servant, though you are Lord of all.
We who believe in you as the Mother of God in truth, may we be delivered by your intercession from eternal fire and darkness, and from the invisible enemies who attack us.
Let us celebrate with praises the Virgin full of grace, as a gate leading to the divine entrance; as the ladder of God, by which one happily ascends; as the certain and secure guide of those who are saved.
Incited by the insanity of my mind, I have committed many sins; it remains now that a fitting punishment should follow me; from which deliver me, O Virgin, while I flee with a firm and stable heart to your divine protection, which is the only safeguard of the faithful.
Tonus Plagius II.
From the Canon of Monday, at Matins.
Together with the martyrs and all the angels, intercede, O chaste one, with the Lord of all for the salvation of those who glorify you with faith.
You have been shown to us as a new paradise of life, O pure one, having the Virgin free from every stain whatsoever; recognizing her, he addressed her, saying:
Hail, joy, holy parent,
Wholly free from stain.
having the tree in the midst, from which Adam, eating, is delivered from evil death.
Since I have spent my life in laziness and evil idleness, O pure one, I flee to your protection; have pity on me, and save me.
Let us praise the heavenly gate, through which all sinners enter into the ways of forgiveness.
Holy Mother of God, sanctify with your compassion my heart defiled by impure deeds.
You brought forth, O Maiden, the Master who put on the form of a servant; pray to him to deliver me from the slavery of the passions, O pure one.
Being more honorable than the angels, you have brought forth the holy God, having been sanctified in a supereminent degree, O Theotokos and Virgin; therefore, guide my soul.
Illumine me who am darkened by sin, O you who have brought forth the light of dispassion, that I may praise you, O pure and Ever-Virgin.
*From the Canon of Tuesday, at Matins.*
You appeared as the gate of life, having destroyed the gates of death by your birth-giving, O pure one; you appeared as a chosen land, through which the mass of mankind was raised up from earth to heaven.
The wicked enemy hunts me constantly with deceit; snatch me from his snares, O all-blameless one, and guide me to do the will of the Master, O Theotokos.
You conceived, O inviolate one, Him who holds and contains the whole world; therefore, I beseech you, deliver me from all crushing punishment.
You were shown to be the throne of God, O Theotokos, upon which he sat in the flesh and raised up humanity from its ancient fall, who therefore praise you with grateful voices.
The seven-lamped candlestick bearing the fire of the knowledge of God, the prophet long ago foresaw in you, O Maiden, shining for those who are in danger in the darkness of ignorance, O all-blameless one; therefore I cry to you, illumine me, I pray.
Do not judge me, O Lord, according to my works, I beseech you, but be merciful to me; she who bore you intercedes with you on my behalf, together with the Baptist.
O unpassable gate, open the gates of true repentance to me, I pray, and show me the path of salvation, O pure one, the guide of all.
Adorned with divine splendors, you brought forth Him who is beautiful in form, O Virgin. Pray to him, therefore, always, that we may be saved.
The malignant enemy constantly hunts me with his deceits and frauds; do you, O most pure one, snatch me from his snares; and guide me prosperously, O Mother of God, so that I may be able to carry out the will of the Lord.
You conceived, O inviolate one, Him who captures and contains the whole world: therefore I beseech you, that you may free me from every punishment of the prison of hell.
Thronus Dei facta es, o Deipara, in quo ille sedens incarnatus, ab antiquo casu erexit homines, qui idcirco te gratis vocibus collaudant.
Prævidit te olim propheta, o Puella ab omni nævo libera (29), candelabrum septem luminibus ornatum, habens ignem divinæ scientiæ, qui lucem præbet iis, qui in ignorantiæ tenebris periclitantur: ideo ad te voces extollo, precorque, illumina me.
O Lord, I beseech you, do not judge me according to my works, but be propitious to me; for which your Mother, the Genitrix, prays to you along with the Baptist.
O gate, through which no way is granted to any man, open to me, I pray, the gates of true repentance, and show me the path of salvation, O Virgin, leader and guide of all.
Surrounded by the varieties of divine splendors, you gave birth to the one beautiful in form, O Virgin. Therefore, always pray to Him that we may be saved.
intercede with constant prayers, that he may deliver from destruction and corruption us, who glorify you with faith and affection.
You became the temple of God, O most immaculate one, in which, when He had dwelt as a priest, He deified human substance and made the faithful His temples in a wondrous way.
We offer to you now a hymn of thanksgiving, O pure Virgin, for through you we who were delivered from the ancient curse have attained the fruit of every blessing.
You brought forth without seed Him whom the Father begat impassibly; and after birth you remained a virgin, just as you were before birth; therefore, grant me beatitude and perpetual glory, O Inviolate one, seeing that you are the Mother of God.
Him, before whom the celestial hosts stand with trembling, you brought forth, O chaste one, united to men through His goodness: entreat Him with intense affection to have mercy on your servants.
You brought forth for us a new infant, the Ancient of days, who demonstrated new paths on earth and renewed our nature worn out by age, O blessed Virgin.
Guard my life in humility, O maiden most dear to God, you who by your birth-giving crushed the arrogance and pride of the demons; and raise me up from the dung-heap of the passions, and satisfy me, hungering for your grace, as I sing: Bless the Lord, all you works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages.
With the voice of the angel we greet you, O most blessed Virgin: Hail, you who contained within yourself God the incomprehensible! Hail, resolution of the curse and introduction of blessing! Hail, O unique one, you who opened the gates of paradise.
Having appeared holier than the Cherubim themselves, O Virgin, in that you brought forth God who is above all holiness, sanctify us all who, with holy voices, sanctify you in faith by night and by day.
*From the Canon of Wednesday, at Matins.*
That you might acquire impassibility for all the sons of Adam, O Lord, you endure suffering upon the cross: the earth trembles and is shaken. But you, O Lady, moved by maternal bowels, burst forth into plaintive and tearful cries.
"O Son, when I brought you forth, I knew you to be fair in beauty above all the sons of men; and how is it, O Christ, that now fixed to the cross you lack beauty?" Thus the Virgin spoke with tears.
You were wounded by the sword of the passion, O Lady, when you beheld the same one crucified, and his most pure side pierced with a lance for our sake.
"When I had conceived in my womb Christ, who alone cannot be contained by the universe, and afterwards brought him forth without pangs; now I experience the pains of childbirth, as I see him lifted up on the cross." Thus the Mother of God said, weeping.
How has the brightness of the Father been lifted up on the cross, he who illuminates all things, and puts to flight the prince of darkness? The all-holy Lady spoke, lamenting as a mother.
"You who are immutable in divinity, you have changed, O Son, hanging on the cross, and you have changed every creature," the Virgin said, and seeing these things, she broke into lamentations, considering with wonder your terrible dispensation.
Jacob foresaw you, O Virgin, as a ladder leading us to heavenly heights, as we had slipped into the abyss of evils; whence we sing hymns to you, O pure one, forever.
You underwent a terrible childbirth, O Lady, in bringing forth Him who, when the creation saw Him willingly crucified on the tree, O long-suffering One, shuddered to behold; yet the blameless one wept, crying out as a mother, whom we magnify.
From the Canon for Thursday at Matins
The Son, co-eternal with the Father, conceived in the end of ages, dwelt in your womb, O Theotokos, for the regeneration of us the faithful, whence we sing hymns to you.
Entreat, O pure one, that the Redeemer be given to us for the absolution of our sins, dispersing the darkness of our souls, O immaculate one, always by your intercessions.
O unmarried Lady, blessed Bearer of God, protect those who sing hymns to you from all harm, for you are the only help of mankind.
He who granted us being, dwelt in your womb, O Theotokos; entreat him for our salvation.
O Virgin, alone pure, full of grace, be my help as I am always tossed by the disturbances of life.
O Lady of all people, most holy Theotokos, completely free me from the slavery of my passions by your intercessions.
We praise you, O Virgin Bearer of God, through whom the curse has been blotted out, and a blessing has come to all who recognize you as the only Mother of our God, who knew no man.
You who gave birth to the Light, enlighten the blinded pupils of my heart, O most praised one, that I who am saved through you may sing your praises, you who are worthy of all praise.
From the Canon of Friday, at Matins.
When the unwed maiden, who is the ornament and glory of the faithful, saw you, the beautiful in form, having neither form nor beauty in your passion, she lamented with maternal affection and glorified you with longing.
When you saw him who was born of you wounded by the lance, your heart was wounded, O most holy and all-pure Virgin; and being struck with amazement, you said: "What did the people, violators of the divine law, render unto you, O my Son?" "I gave birth to you without the passion of pain; and how do I see you now consorting with passions? How do you bear this, O eternal Son?" cried the Virgin; "I glorify your long-suffering."
You have sprouted, O Virgin, from the root of Jesse, and like a flower you have brought forth the Creator of the world, who caused the shoots of that same world to wither by the divine plant of the cross, which he accepted in the flesh.
When the ewe lamb saw the Shepherd and Master lifted up on the wood, erupting into lamentations as a mother, she cried out: "What is this new mystery, O Son? How, being immortal in essence, have you accepted death, wishing to redeem mortals from corruption?"
"You are crucified voluntarily, O eternal Son," the Theotokos exclaimed; "and as I look upon this, my soul is now plunged into a baptism of pain and grief. For you die, providing life to those who sing: Blessed are you, O God, etc."
"O most high and eternal Son, you endure spittle, reproaches, and the cross, struck in mockery with a reed," the Theotokos cried out; "I glorify your great patience, whom children bless, priests praise, and peoples exalt throughout all ages."
You carry, O inviolate Lady, him who carries all things, and you hold in your hands as an infant him who delivered us from the hand of the warring enemy; and you look upon him who delivered us from the deepest abyss of wickedness being lifted up upon the wood of the Cross.
From the Canon of Saturday, at Matins.
O holier and purer than the Cherubim and Seraphim
You have been revealed as having brought forth the Maker of the creation, whom unceasingly entreat to pity your servants, those who glorify you.
Women, burning with love and desire for Christ, stood around you, o immaculate Lady, who ineffably bore Him, and danced with joyous intent.
The Lord, O Immaculate One, having ineffably put on flesh from you, received the catalog of women who strove with manly courage.
Appear as our deliverer from every harm of the enemy, O Lady, entreating Christ, who alone has bowels of mercy.
You who in the flesh brought forth Christ, mortify the passions of my flesh, and quicken my soul, O Virgin, by your intercession.
He who showed you to be a pure virgin even after childbirth has caused the choirs of holy women to follow you in dance.
The choir of women loved you entirely as being near to God, O Virgin, and following after you were presented to the Lord of all, calling you blessed with one accord, O Immaculate One.
O you who brought forth the Light, illumine, O Virgin, the darkness of my soul, dispelling the very heavy darkness of sin.
TONUS GRAVIS
From the Canon of the second day, at Matins.
O Virgin—all pure, the protection of the faithful, the conversion of the sinful to God—save me by your intercession.
Only you having brought forth the heavenly King, O Virgin, cast down the sin that reigns within me, and having pity, save me.
O chaste Virgin, full of divine grace, fill my mind with grace and perfectly deliver it from the darkness of ignorance.
Through you, O most holy one, the Lord, having done great things for you, was incarnate from you beyond reason. Entreat Him, therefore, earnestly for all.
We praise you, O Maiden worthy of all praise, who brought forth the Word worthy of all praise, whom all the powers of heaven praise with voices that never fall silent.
The bush prefigured you, O chaste one, in that it did not burn; for you have brought forth the Fire that cannot be endured, O Virgin, therefore I cry to you: Burn up my material passions.
The human mass was loosed from the curse by your childbirth, O Virgin; for you gave birth to the All-Blessed One who adorns all things with blessings.
With the illuminating splendors of the Word who has risen from you like a sun, O chaste Virgin, enlighten me as I am covered in the darkness of sins and passions.
From the Canon of Tuesday, for Matins.
Heal my soul, corrupted and grievously affected by many sins, O Virgin Mother of God, that I may always glorify you with intense feeling in voices of thanksgiving.
You have brought forth, O most holy one, the Incomprehensible Word, who conversed with us in the flesh according to his hypostasis; pray to him with your ceaseless supplications for the salvation of those who always call you blessed.
The prophet foresaw you as the Gate of God, through which he alone passed, as he knows how, O immaculate Virgin; therefore I entreat you, open to me the gates of repentance.
O pure and blessed Lady, who in an inexplicable way gave birth to God; together with his divine Baptist, pray incessantly for us who fall into the miseries of this life and are held fast by the bonds of sins.
The Lord was incarnate from your pure blood, granting repentance to all men through your intercessions.
The shadows of the Law demonstrated through many figures that you, O most pure one, would give birth to God; entreat him to deliver me from all iniquity and from the passions of the body.
O luminous cloud of the sun, send forth to me the ray of the spiritual light of true repentance, and dispel the darkness of wicked thoughts, so that with faith I may praise you as the salvation of the faithful.
Mortify the earthly wisdom of my flesh, O chaste and inviolate Mother of God, who gave birth to Life, who by your divine power utterly abolished death through death.
Bring me up, O most benign one, from the abyss of negligence and unbridled passions, you who gave birth to the abyss of most pious mercy, and grant me a fountain of tears.
As rain the Word descended into your womb; whom I beseech you, O most holy Virgin, to entreat to dry up the streams of my measureless evils.
new scroll the prophet saw you to be, in which the Word of the Father was written; therefore, O chaste one, I beseech you, entreat Jesus, the Renovator of the universe, that he might write me in the book of the living.
Having a soul destroyed by the disease of most grievous passions, I suppliantly ask you, O Lady, who by your birth-giving did destroy Hades, quicken me through acts of repentance.
O Mother of God, firm protection and hope of Christians, on that awesome day be an advocate for me who have committed many sins, and deliver me from the dreadful gehenna, numbering me with the sheep that shall be placed on the right hand.
Having received within your bowels the fire of intolerable burning, O Virgin, you didst undergo no combustion. Therefore, snatch me from the
unquenchable fire, sprinkling upon me now the dew of true repentance, through the beautiful modes by which my soul may be adorned.
The Lord, having put on the garment of my flesh, came forth from you, O chaste One. Entreat Him, therefore, O Virgin, to adorn me with a robe of light, now that I have been stripped of the most heavy sackcloth of my passions.
O you who gave birth to the divine Light, filled with grace, enlighten my soul, darkened as it is by transgressions; deliver me, I pray, from eternal darkness, that I may magnify and glorify you, O ever-blessed One.
From the Canon of Wednesday, at Matins.
The bond of Adam is torn asunder, cried out the all-pure Lady, as You, O Son, are wounded by the lance; therefore, I praise Your passion, O Master, from which a fountain of impassibility has flowed unto all.
Beholding the voluntary Lamb who came to the passion to be sacrificed, the mother and lamb, shedding fountains of tears, said: What is this, O holy Child? How do You undergo death, You who prepare to bring to life all who have died?
Offer suppliant prayers for us who sing your praises, O Theotokos, that we may find help in the time of adversity, as we now glorify the passion of your Son.
With what eyes shall I look upon the eye that dries up all the abysses, O my Son, as it is extinguished while You hang upon the Cross? The chaste Virgin cried out, convulsing herself with laments.
You bring forth a new Infant, O all-blameless Theotokos, Him who is understood to be before the ages and timelessly united with the Father, who by His Cross has restored to newness the human race, which had grown old in sins by the counsel of the prince of evil.
How shall I not mourn, O sweetest Child, while beholding You upon the Cross? How shall I not wail, while You, O most just Judge, suffer unjustly? Thus the Virgin Mother spoke.
The ark of the Law formerly prefigured you, O pure One, which bore within it not the two tablets, but Christ Himself, the Lawgiver, whom the lawless people nailed to the Cross, who saves us as we cry: All you works of the Lord, bless the Lord and exalt Him throughout all ages.
Your Son, O all-immaculate One, shone forth as an intelligible Light upon the Cross, and made a spectacle of the princes of darkness, O all-blameless One; He darkened the brightness of the sun and enlightened the multitude of the faithful.
From the double Canon of Thursday, at Matins.
O immaculate Theotokos, with the bodiless angels, prophets, martyrs, and apostles, intercede for us, that we may obtain forgiveness of trespasses and abundant mercy, O Virgin.
He who made the immaterial spirits His ministers, has been born of you, O Theotokos, and for His ineffable mercy is revealed to human eyes.
OF SAINT JOSEPH THE HYMNOGRAPHER
Thou who established the heavenly powers by the Word; who hast granted grace to the prophets, and to the disciples, and to all the martyrs; by the intercessions of these, O God, and of thy immaculate Mother, save all, and have mercy, as one most compassionate and most kind.
O tongs, which didst receive the divine coal in thy holy womb without any burning whatsoever, consume, O most chaste one, our transgressions.
O elect Mother of God, together with the holy apostles, martyrs, and prophets, pray that I may be able to be freed from dangers, tribulations, and sins.
O most immaculate one, who in the flesh gavest birth to Him who is without time, entreat Him to free us from temporal passions, we who celebrate thy praises with hymns.
O Lord my God, who wert born of a virgin who knew no marriage, by her supplications and those of thy apostles, grant me remission of sins, and deliver me from future punishment.
O Mary, Lady of all creation, deliver my humble heart from the enemy who shamelessly seeks to rule over it, O Mother of God, deliver me.
Thou who gavest birth to the Light, enlighten my soul, darkened by passions, and entreat, together with the apostles, prophets, and martyrs, that I may be saved from all sin, from all harm, and from all the wickedness of the enemy.
Heal, O most immaculate one, my wretched soul, incurably sick from the deceits of this life and from the flooding of many sins.
Together with the angels, apostles, and martyrs, O most immaculate one, entreat thy Son and Lord, that thy servants may be delivered from temptations, dangers, and afflictions.
O chaste Mother of God, praiseworthy in every respect, every tongue with faith glorifies thee, who art the glory and ornament of our race, and the guide of the wandering.
Thou gavest birth to the Fire and didst remain unconsumed, O Virgin who knew no marriage; therefore entreat Him whom thou didst bear, together with the choirs of the bodiless ones, and the apostles, that those who faithfully chant may be saved: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord, praise and superexalt Him unto all ages.
Every tongue praises thee, glorifying God who is su-
(32). It is opportune in this eulogy of S. Joseph for the Blessed Virgin, that Bartholomew of Pisa, in *De laudibus Virginis*, lib. i, fruct. 7, says: "A wise man ought to choose what is most perfect and better: but Christ is uncreated Wisdom, and since it is better and more choiceworthy to have a Mother without any stain than with a stain, and He could have her so. Therefore, etc." Likewise, it is also opportune that Bernardino de' Busti, in *Mariali*, serm. 1, on the Conception, part iii, says: "Since Christ from eternity chose the B. Virgin to be His Mother, according to that which is sung, 'God chose her, and forechose her,' it is to be concluded that He, to whom all future things were present, willed to preserve her from all evils and the damages of original sin, so that He might fulfill the debt of a good Son." And in the same place, serm. 2, part iii: "Those whom God chooses for some task, He so prepares and disposes, that for that [task]—as the Apostle in II Cor. says: 'He made us fit ministers of the New Testament.' For a wise regulator ought to regulate those things which are directed toward an end according to the order of that end itself, according to the dictum of the philosopher in Metaphysics, and it is proved by c. *Prodest* 23. q. because a prince, when assuming someone for his office, is said to wish to dispense with him in the best way he can. Likewise: He who wills the consequent also wills every necessary antecedent; and it is a crime of sacrilege to doubt whether he is worthy whom an emperor has chosen. But the glorious Virgin Mary was chosen divinely from eternity to be the Mother of God, she herself saying that in Ecclesiasticus: 'From the beginning and before the ages I was created.' And Proverbs viii: 'I was ordained from eternity, and from of old, before the earth was,' all of which the Church attributes to the B. Virgin. Therefore God, through His grace, preserved her fit for so wonderful a work." So he.
O Bride who brought forth the All-Praiseworthy, Virgin of God, entreat Him incessantly to save our souls.
Having you, O Pure One, in their midst as a most luminous lamp, the choirs of martyrs and apostles, shedding the light of divine gifts, are spiritually illuminated; and they, in turn, illuminate all those by whom you are glorified.
You carry in your holy arms Him who carries all things; entreat Him, O Pure One, that He may preserve us unharmed by the malice of the alien.
From the Canon for Friday at Matins
When you saw, O Lady, Him who had risen from you, hanging on the cross out of the excess of His mercy, you lamented, while yet glorifying Him.
Grant us, O Pure Virgin, forgiveness of our debts through your maternal intercessions, you who gave birth to God the Word, who was made man and fastened to the cross.
You gave birth, O Virgin, without seed, to the Word, who by His cross and in His goodness destroyed corruption.
God sanctified your womb, O holy and venerable one, dwelling within it; and, being exalted on the cross, He exalted all creation together with Himself.
You hymn Him who was incarnate of you, and you lament as you behold Him exalted on the cross, O Mother and Virgin, pure and altogether spotless.
When the All-Holy One saw the All-Holy Word, who had risen from her like the sun, exalted on the holy cross and sanctifying mortals, she broke into lamentations.
The hosts of the saints entreat, O most chaste Maiden, the Lord who came forth from your womb, who showed them on the cross the path of courageous struggle; and they glorify you as the Queen of all.
O Friend of the good, holier than the Cherubim, you who brought forth in the flesh God the Word, who voluntarily exalted His flesh on the cross—earnestly intercede with Him on behalf of all.
From the Canon for Saturday at Matins
O Most Holy One, who brought forth God, the all-holy Word, sanctify all who glorify you with faith.
The Word, never departing from the bosom of the Father, preserved her from all the turpitude of sin. For who is there among men who, if he could fashion his own bride, would not create her without every stain? Thus did He.
in your bosom as an infant He was seen being held, He who is without origin, having received an origin from you, O immaculate one. Beseech the God whom you have borne, O all-praised one, to deliver your flock from every kind of temptation and tribulation. O most holy Virgin, you who have become the indissoluble bridal chamber of God, entreat Him, O all-holy Virgin, that I may become an inhabitant of the spiritual bridal chamber. O most holy Virgin, you who have borne the most holy Word incarnate, whom every spirit praises and sanctifies, sanctify your servants. Let us venerate the Theotokos with hymns, as more honorable than the angels. The assembly of all women, who by practicing virtue and by struggling have sought the Lord, O all-undefiled one, ceaselessly beseeches your divine countenance. Being prone to sin, I tremble at the terrible judgment seat of Him who was born of you, O pure one; in which, O gracious one, preserve me uncondemned.
PLAGAL FOURTH TONE
From the Canon of Tuesday, at Matins.
Apply your healing hand to me, as I am gravely ill with the deadly disease of bodily and spiritual passions, O pure one, so that in faith I may always call you blessed. I possess you, O all-holy one, as my guardian in temptations; after God, I have you as my most vigilant protection; may I find you, O all-pure one, to be my deliverer from every sentence of condemnation on the day of judgment. I beseech you with faith, as the pure receptacle of the Lord, O Mother of God; purify me from every stain, and show me to be a house of the pure and divine Spirit. Put to death, O pure one, the movements of my passions, you who by your childbirth killed the living sin of the first ancestor, O Maiden. So that with voices of thanksgiving I may always glorify you, O all-undefiled one; dispel the darkness of my soul, and with the light of repentance illuminate me, who am darkened by sins. The God Word of God, having dwelt in your womb, O full of divine grace, shows you to be the protection of all who are weary and who cry out: Blessed are You, the God of our fathers, etc. You, O Virgin, are the firmness of those who stand; and to those who have fallen,
you are a restoration, O Virgin; wherefore raise me up now that I have collapsed, that I may glorify you, O full of grace.
Illumine, I pray, O portal of light, the eyes of my heart, which the thickest darkness of sin has obscured; and send forth to me, O Lady, a ray of repentance; and by your intercessions, O chaste Virgin, deliver me from eternal fire.
*From the Canon for Tuesday, at Matins.*
Show me the ways of repentance, O Virgin, and turn me away from the path that leads me to sin, that I may praise you, who are worthy of all praise and most blessed.
Restoration of those who are prostrate, O Theotokos, raise me up from the abyss of my evils, and steady me, O Lady, upon the rock of God's commandments.
Most praiseworthy Lady, we extol your praises with loud voices: fill my mind with all joy; granting me profitable sorrow, opportune repentance, and saving knowledge.
Holy Theotokos, who gave birth to the holy Word—who alone rests in all the saints—sanctify my mind, which ever persists in wicked deeds.
The manna-containing urn, O Theotokos, once prefigured you; for you brought forth Christ, the manna of knowledge, showering it upon all who honor you.
Above all causes and reasons, you gave birth to the cause and author of the universe, who, because of his superabundant kindness, became man. Whence, O pure one, we call you blessed with one accord.
As I am tossed about on the sea of sin, O chaste one, by your intercession guide me, I pray, O Lady, to the most tranquil harbors of saving repentance; that I, who am always enveloped in the darkness of sloth, may at last behold the light of salvation.
With the lightning rays of your Son and God, who came forth from your womb, illumine and gladden, O most immaculate one, those who praise you with faith; and by your intercession deliver us from the darkest gloom of Hades and from eternal torment.
*From the Canon for Wednesday, at Matins.*
Standing by your cross, O Lord, the unwed one, seeing the wounds within you, O Master, was herself wounded and cried out: Woe is me, my Son! I escaped the pains of childbirth when you were born; but now I am pressed and tormented by pains.
That widely-praised ewe, seeing the Lamb drawn to voluntary passion and crucified in the flesh, lamented with tears while glorifying His long-suffering.
The blameless heifer, seeing her own calf suspended upon the wood, cried out with a wail: My Son, how could a people of transgressors have no pity on you, who had pity on them, but chose instead to kill you unjustly through fraudulent counsel
strange spectacle I behold, you cried out, O most praiseworthy, how you, who shake the whole earth with a look, having been lifted up upon the wood, have fallen asleep, you who are about to awaken those who have slept from the ages? I worship your patience, O Son.
I once gave birth, O Son, to the Lord of life, and the most beautiful in form beyond the sons of men, the Virgin cried out, and how do you die now, O Son, having no form or beauty while suspended on the cross, you who adorn all things with a nod?
O Word, Sun that knows no setting, the Virgin said, how have you now died, stretched out upon the wood? And the sun, seeing this, departed from its course, unable to shine while you, O Master, were suffering. I hymn and worship your divine power.
Sighing in pain, wailing with maternal affection, and unable to suppress the constriction and anguish of her heart, with her eyes fixed on the cross toward Him who was born from your womb, you cried out: O Son, what is this spectacle? How do you suffer, you who are by nature impassible, and fully will to deliver the race of mankind from corruption?
Having fled the pains of mothers at the time when I gave birth to you, O most mild Son, but now while I see you voluntarily embracing the passion and undergoing pains, my heart is torn, and my soul is filled with sorrows. Thus cried out she who is most pure, whom we rightfully magnify.
298
From the Canon for Thursday, at Matins.
Beyond the laws of nature, you gave birth, O most undefiled one, to the Lawgiver and God, made man; entreat Him, as one who is good, to forgive us the things in which we have transgressed against the law, while we cry out unceasingly: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He has been gloriously magnified.
Entreat, O Virgin, Him who is good, that He may grant me release from sins and deliverance from the most grievous adversities of this life.
Grant me strength, O inviolate one, that I may be able to cultivate heavenly thoughts in my soul, and direct its movements toward the entrances of life.
Since you are full of the waters of the life-flowing stream, O Maiden, grant a drink to all of us who praise you with a pious and religious mind.
You have become a palace of the most high King, and a throne of fiery form, higher, O Virgin, than the Cherubim and Seraphim in an ineffable way; wherefore every spirit extols you with praises as the Mother of the Creator.
You repressed the assault of death while giving birth to the immortal God; Him entreat, O chaste one, to mortify the passions of my miserable body, and to deem me worthy of life.
Offer your supplication to the Lord, that He may have mercy and grant salvation to those who take refuge in you, O most holy Theotokos, help of mankind.
Since you are the dwelling of light, enlighten, O Virgin, the
darkened soul of mine with the gloom of my misdeeds, that having faith and longing, I may ever sing your glory.
From the Canon of Friday, at Matins
When the blameless one saw Him who sees all things hanging on the Cross, she cried out in lamentation: "What is this, my Son? What have they rendered unto You, they who have enjoyed so many of Your gifts? How shall I endure this pain? Glory be to Your wondrous dispensation, O most merciful and long-suffering One."
"Beyond the laws of men I gave birth to You, O Son; and how do those who transgress the law lift You up on the wood in the midst of lawless men, You who alone established the laws of life?" Thus the Theotokos spoke, lamenting in song.
"You brought forth as a new infant Him who is before all ages and perfect, and on the Cross, O Lady altogether pure, He makes all things perfect in His goodness."
"You have healed us who were sick with sin, O Virgin, by giving birth to the Savior and physician of all, who, wounded on the wood of the Cross, O undefiled one, bestows incorruption upon our souls."
"A sword has pierced your heart, seeing your Son, O undefiled Lady, nailed to the wood, and bearing sufferings, and having His divine side pierced by the spear according to His own will, and in it laying low and slaying the hostile dragon; whence, mourning as a mother, you magnified Him."
"I am filled with sorrow, seeing You, O Son, suffering unjustly; my soul is wounded as Your side is pierced by the spear," the Theotokos cried out, weeping and lamenting, the only Lady, whom we all worthily call blessed, and piously proclaim: "Blessed are You, O God of our fathers."
"An intellectual cloud, and tabernacle of sanctification, a throne of God and gate, a lampstand of light, and the dawning of the Word, all of us the faithful name you, O most pure Virgin, since you are the blessed Mother of Him who fashioned all things."
"You were revealed as a light-filled bridal chamber, O Virgin, for Him who dwelt in your undefiled womb, and who voluntarily endured a blessed passion, and granted impassibility to all through His ineffable mercy; whom, adoring with faith, we piously magnify."
C 
 D 
(35) The expression "immaculate," when it implies the absence of all stain, speaks of no limitation, and extends to all times and moments of life, just as that which is "infinite" simply and absolutely spoken admits of no end from either extreme; nor could you properly call that "immense" which can be subject to any measure. But rather, if these terms are used of a thing which in any way admits the form which is negated by those terms, anyone would affirm that the locution is hyperbolic and improper. In the same way, the expression "immaculate," when it speaks of the absence of all stain throughout every difference of time, and is applied to the Blessed Virgin in a proper sense, and not a hyperbolic one, as the common body of doctors admits, undoubtedly comprehends her innocence within the bosom of its meaning for all the moments of her life. How acutely this absence of stain for every difference of time was grasped by the most wise Idiota in his book *On the Contemplation of the Blessed Virgin*, explaining that place in Canticles 4: "Thou art all fair, my love, and there is no spot in thee," saying: "Thou art all fair," he says, "O most glorious Virgin, not in part, but in the whole; and the stain of sin, whether mortal, or venial, or original, is not in you, nor ever was, nor shall be." From which it is evident that the word "immaculate" brings with it innocence and exception from every sin for every difference of time. See Vega in *Theologia Mariana*, Palæstra 4, Certamine 3, n. 510.
OF ST. JOSEPH THE HYMNOGRAPHER
From the Canon of Saturday, at Matins.
The chorus of women, knowing you to be the Mother of the Lord, O Virgin, wrestled strenuously behind you so as to offer themselves to the same Lord.
Jesus, who can be contained nowhere, inhabited your sanctified womb without any restriction of narrowness, O inviolate Virgin.
You gave birth to the divine Lawgiver, who is above the laws of nature, O you who are immune from every stain (36): He who reformed the lost nature of mankind.
You abolished the condemnation of our first parents, O chaste one, when you gave birth in the flesh to the Reconciler of all and only Lord.
The splendor of the Father, O chaste one, took up its dwelling in you, and with the immaterial rays of his Divinity dissipated the darkness of polytheism and illuminated the world.
As you were entirely pure, so did you receive in your womb the Word becoming incarnate; implore him, O all-pure one, to cleanse me of my faults, as I take refuge in you with a pure faith, both in soul and body.
Having appeared, O Virgin, dispel the darkest clouds of my soul with your illumination, you who gave birth to the Sun of Justice.
You who bore the fullness of good things in your arms, O all-pure one, fulfill the intercessions of your servants, and guide our paths toward God, strengthening us to live according to virtue.
FROM THE TRIODION
From the Canon of Sunday on which the Gospel of the parable of the Prodigal Son is read.
God, demonstrating all kindness in me; forgive me, as you are beneficent, the multitude of my transgressions, moved by the divine prayers of your Mother.
O fairest among women; enrich also me, who have become a beggar due to my many sins, with the beauty of good things, that I may glorify thee, O chaste one. O help of men; O firmest hope of all Christians; O refuge of those who attain to salvation: save me, O inviolate Virgin, by thy maternal intercessions; and make me worthy of the life to come. A O Virgin all-holy, full of grace (39-40), who gavest birth to the propitiation for all, lighten the heavy burden of my offenses by thy prayers. O damsel, who brought forth Christ the Lord and Savior, make me worthy of salvation, who have been stripped of all good things, O chaste Virgin, that I may praise thy greatness (41). O Mother of God, who didst appear more brilliant than the Cherubim and Seraphim and all the heavenly host, together with them beseech the divine Word whom thou didst embody, O all-undefiled one, from the Father who has no beginning, that we may all be made worthy of eternal good things. O chaste Mother of God, the only restoration of those who have fallen, restore even me, since I am wholly broken and humbled by manifold sins.
It is customary for the office to be recited in the churches of the Greeks from Septuagesima, which they call the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, until Holy Saturday. See more concerning the Triodion in the work of the most erudite Leo Allatius, *De libris ecclesiasticis Græcorum*, dissert. 1, from page 69 onwards. Furthermore, the *Theotocia* which I have brought into this work from the Greek Triodion are not only excerpts from the Triodion printed in Greek in Venice, but from a manuscript Greek Triodion which exists in the very ancient codex of the most celebrated Barberini library, number 118, in which certain things are found under the name of our Joseph which are missed in the printed Triodion.
(39-40) If the Virgin is all-holy, then she is always devoid of every sin and stain. For holiness, according to Dionysius the Areopagite in the book *On the Divine Names*, chap. 12, "is free from all wickedness, and perfect, and in all respects uncontaminated," or, as others read, "immaculate purity." Which definition St. Thomas proves in 3, d. 3. q. 5, 2, 1, in c. Perinde. It is, therefore, the same for the Mother of God to be called "all-holy" by St. Joseph as it is to be called expert of every stain and in every respect immaculate. See Velasquez, *De Maria immaculate concepta*, book IV, dissert. 2, annot. 4, nn. 6 and 7, where he proves that the word "holy" is much more seasonable and more significant for expressing the pure and unblemished conception of Mary, and that which does not know any guilt, than the word "immaculate," because besides uncontaminated purity, or together with it as a consequent property or an effect following from the cause, it also signifies a thing dedicated and consecrated to God and to the uses of God, and therefore protected by a certain religion against injury and profane force, and which is inviolate in every observance, as both jurists and authors of the Latin language observe. Thus the jurisconsult Marcianus said in l. 1, *De rer. divisione*: "That is properly called holy which is defended and protected from the injury of men": so that he seems to have defined the Blessed Virgin as "all-holy," that is, defended and protected from the injury of Adam in her conception by divine grace. Furthermore, while St. Joseph called the Blessed Virgin absolutely "all-holy," he manifestly indicated that she had been such in every difference of time, and therefore also in the first instant of her conception; and those who opine that she contracted original sin at that time are compelled to assert that she was at that time not only not "all-holy," but wholly infected with sin and stain.
(41) Father Joan. Baptista Novatus, in his work *De eminentia Deiparæ*, cap. 5, q. 5, asking whether they the more praise the Virgin who preach her immaculate conception than those who celebrate her other great deeds, privileges, prerogatives, and graces freely given, says: The Mother of God is more praised when she is preached to have been preserved from original sin than when her other prerogatives or graces freely given (such as the accelerated use of reason, an optimal bodily temperament, a beautiful and elegant form, preservation from all infirmity and corruption after death, a swift resurrection, the glorification of the body, infused virtues, the spirit of prophecy, etc.) are recounted. It is proved: because the fact that the Blessed Virgin was not for even a moment deprived of sanctifying grace is a greater good of hers and a greater excellence than many prerogatives and graces freely given which were in that Virgin; because to posit the Virgin as maculated with original sin even for a moment is to constitute her spiritually dead, inimical to God, and bound to the servitude of Satan: but this is clearly a greater evil than the lack of all the prerogatives and graces freely given: for by this lack she would never have been formally constituted spiritually dead, inimical to God, and bound to Satanic servitude. But he who preaches that the Virgin was preserved from original sin affirms that she did not lack sanctifying grace even for a moment. Therefore, the Virgin is more praised when she is preached as preserved from original sin than when her other prerogatives or graces freely given are recounted, because in this way her greater good and greater excellence are commended.
(42) Since the Cherubim and Seraphim and all the angelic spirits were created by God in grace, the Blessed Virgin would certainly not have appeared more splendid than these if, when she first appeared, namely in the very instant of her conception, she had been found not only without the light of grace, but with the darkness of sin. And although St. Joseph would have said enough to express the immunity of the Mother of God from original stain if he had said that the Mother of God herself, when she first appeared, had appeared as splendid in grace as the Cherubim and Seraphim and all the angelic spirits: yet he deliberately said that the Mother of God appeared more splendid than the angels, because almighty God, with the Holy Spirit cooperating, according to His wisdom, power, goodness, and kindness, preparing Mary’s soul with such purity, sanctity, and splendor of grace as was befitting the Mother of His Only-Begotten, for a necessary reason made her much more splendid, excellent, and sublime than the heavenly spirits. See Velasquez, *De Maria immaculate concepta*, book 1, Dissert. 2, annot. 2.
By your enlightening intercessions, O Virgin, enlighten my intellectual eyes, darkened by malice, I pray; and lead me to the ways of repentance, so that I may dutifully hymn you, who clothed the ineffable Word in a body.
*From the Canon on Meat-fare Sunday, at Vespers.*
Darkened by the night of transgressions, I now take refuge with intense affection, O Lady, to you who brought forth for those dwelling in darkness Christ, the Sun of righteousness; save me.
The limits of nature have been overcome in you, O most chaste Virgin; for you have remained, having brought forth God without male copulation, immaculate after birth just as you were before birth, O chaste one.
O Virgin worthy of all praise, who alone are the most firm aid of the faithful before God, deliver me from darkness and from the punishment prepared for those living wickedly.
Do not cease to implore God on our behalf, who is good and alone knows our infirmity; that the city which honours you may be delivered from pestilence, famine, earthquake, and every adversity, O Theotokos, the hope of earth-born men.
*From the Canon of the third day of Cheese-fare [week].*
O Virgin, to be celebrated with all praise, be for us the way of repentance, leading us to the gates of salvation.
O Maiden, who did make our earthly mass heavenly: make my soul's earthly and corrupt furrow fruitful in virtues by the rain of your mercies, while it cries out and superexalts Christ unto the ages.
We, piously and religiously honouring you as a strong fortress and firm hope, beseech you: implore your Son, O Virgin, that we may be saved.
*From the Canon of the fourth day, at Matins.*
Quiet the tumult of my passions; heal the wounds of my soul; rouse me from the sleep of lethargy by your intercession and your protection, O most holy Queen, Mother and Virgin.
Open to us, O Virgin, who knock and call upon your mercy, O most ready refuge of men, and firm aid in all things, and strongest protection in necessities.
By your most swift intercession, your most vigilant protection, your most strong aid, O chaste Lady, preserve now your faithful servants from every hostile assault, and save us from passions, and faults, and temptations.
*From the Canon of the fourth day, at Vespers.*
You conceived ineffably and brought forth supernaturally, O Mother of God, the Lord of creation; whom you must ceaselessly implore that we, who call you blessed, may be delivered from all wrath.
O Lady over all, receiving the supplications of your servants, O holy Theotokos, deliver me from darkness and from the unquenchable fire: blot out the multitude of my evils by your mediation, and by your unique intercession.
Since God has established you as an aid to the human race, do not fail, O chaste Virgin, to pray fervently to Him on behalf of us the faithful.
From the canon for Thursday, at Vespers.
O holy Virgin, the only succor of the faithful, grant the aid of your intercession in the time of the fast to all who recognize you as the chaste Mother of God.
As the cause of all good things, O Lady, we all earnestly beseech you that we may enter into the struggle of the fast with your mediation, and attain to a saving end of the same.
O pure propitiatory for those who sin, O holy Lady, by your intercession tear up the written bond of my sins.
We who are about to enter the gates of the fast beseech you, the gate of God, to come with your servants, O Lady, and to widen completely our minds and thoughts so as to perform saving desires.
With your lightning-like rays, O good Lady of the world, dispel the darkness of my soul and the night of my sin, so that I may joyfully praise and bless you.
O you alone who strengthened the weakness of human existence by your divine childbirth, O chaste one, appear as a helper to me as I enter into the divine stadium of the fast.
O sole aid of mankind, be a helper to your servants in the time of abstinence, so that serving well-pleasingly in repentance, we may receive the kingdom of the heavens.
O all-chaste Mother of God, to you we flee: have mercy on us by your good intercession, and render your Son and Lord propitious to all of us in this time of the fast now beginning for the salvation of the faithful who celebrate you with hymns unto all ages.
Raising up of the falling, mediatrix for sinners, refreshment of travelers, consolation of the sorrowful, O all-holy Virgin, dispel the sorrow of my soul, O chaste one, and beseech God for comfort to be given to me from above, as I sing joyfully and exalt you unto all ages.
O lover of goodness, Virgin, who gave birth to the good Savior, make us all good, O Lady—we who are harmed by immeasurable passions and thoughts, and burdened by unbearable loads of sins—so that we may rightly glorify you as the Theotokos.
O protection of all who trust in you, who are a vigilant protectress in necessities, O Theotokos, deliver us from eternal hell and from the bitter torments already prepared, so that we may dutifully sing of your wonders.
Tone II.
From the canon for the Monday of the first week of Lent.
You who gave birth to the fountain of dispassion, heal me who am worn out by the wounds of the passions, O Maiden, and...
from eternal fire snatch me away, you who alone are most full of the grace of God.
You, O chaste one, who have rendered our earthly and corruptible substance heavenly, through your fervent intercession cause our prayers and supplications to be directed toward your own and all men’s God and King.
Heal my miserable soul, enfeebled by all the assaults of the wicked demons, O immaculate Lady, you who have given birth to Christ the physician, our redemption: for we have recognized you, O Maiden, as a Virgin incorrupt.
TONE II. From the canon for Tuesday of the same week.
O earth, immune from the hand of the plowman, who brought forth the Nourisher of the universe, who opening His hand by His own good pleasure fills every living creature with divine power: strengthen with life-giving bread our hearts, weakened and crushed by the weight of our difficult transgressions.
O chaste Mother of God, heal the sores of my soul and the passions of my heart, and the wanderings of my mind, as you are the only help for sinners and a wall for those who are being plundered.
Who, having heard, shall offer praises to you, who gave birth beyond understanding to the praiseworthy and Sovereign Lord, O chaste one, whom the angelic ranks above praise? Entreat Him, therefore, on behalf of a people who have sinned, O Virgin who has not known man.
TONE II. From the canon for Wednesday of the same week.
You who exercise dominion over all creatures, O Lady; for since you gave birth to the Lord himself, deliver me from the slavery of the crafty one who is the only enemy.
You the shining lamp and lantern, in which the fire of the Divinity dwelt, have illuminated those who in the night...
of corruption were held fast, O you whom we all venerate, O immaculate and blessed one, celebrating your offspring with praises.
O you who brought forth Christ the Lord, a most just Judge and the only one easily reconciled, O Virgin maiden, deliver me from judgment, and from fire, and from the punishment which the pleasure of sin has procured for me.
*From the Canon of the fifth day of the same week.*
I will cry out day and night, being afflicted, to you, and I shall be saved, and I shall pass over the wall of pleasures by your aid, O Virgin, being fortified by your power.
You remained unburned, having brought forth the fire of the Divinity, O Virgin, but burn up the passions of the souls of those who with faith cry out to you, using the voice of the angel, O only cause of joy.
You who have deified our earthly clay through the indwelling of God in you, O most pure one, deliver all from dangers.
*From the Canon of the Friday of the same week.*
O pure illumination of my darkened soul, the salvation of my weakness, save me, save me who am perishing, O Maiden, and clothe me with the tunic of incorruption, corrupted as I am by most grievous transgressions.
I have spent my life in negligence and sloth, and my soul has been weighed down by the sleep of sin; but I run to your ever-watchful intercession: do not allow me, O Virgin Theotokos, to fall asleep in death.
Wretched me, defiled in soul and body by the filth of irrational actions (I pray to you with crying and sighs, O Mother of God, utterly lost as I am), cleanse me by your intercessory prayer; and have mercy on me, and save me.
*From the Canon of the second day of the second week.*
O immaculate tabernacle, most holy Virgin, cleanse me, defiled by the allurements of most grievous passions, at the fountain of your mercy: and grant me showers of compunction, O Lady, destroying the deep whirlpool of sin.
The Most High, dwelling in your womb, showed you to be more spacious than the heavens, and an unassailable protection for those who cry out, O Virgin: All you works, bless and praise the Lord.
O Virgin Mary, Lady of the universe, deliver me, who have been enslaved by all kinds of sins, O Virgin; for you ineffably brought forth the Liberator of our souls, the essential Goodness.
*From the Canon of the third day of the second week.*
O you who alone brought forth the eternal Word, O most immaculate one, [(45)] [who are] alone pure.
Since you remained a Virgin after birth, having brought forth him, who from you was incarnate without your changing, O unwedded one, intercede for all, that our life may be delivered from corruption. Let us, O faithful, bless the Virgin, the mountain untouched by the hand of the stonecutter, the light-bearing lampstand, the ladder by which we most happily ascend, the dwelling place of God. We beseech you, O Virgin, who are the royal chariot, the bright cloud, the most fertile mountain, the curdled mountain, free from marital union, to heal the passions of our souls. *From the Canon of the fourth day of the second week, for Matins.* O you who are the restoration of those who have fallen, raise me up, O pure one, who have been brought down by the precipice of pleasures, and have fallen into the abysses of grievous sins, and lead me toward life. O Virgin, who without any cultivation brought forth the heavenly ear of corn, which nourishes all things by divine power, fill my hungry, humble soul. Since I have been plunged into the depths of wickedness, and am tossed by the storms of contrary thoughts, obeying demons, and enslaved to pleasures, help me, O pure Virgin-Mother, guiding me on the path of salvation. *From the Canon of the fifth day, for Matins.* You who lent flesh to Christ from your own flesh, mortify the passions of my flesh, O pure Virgin who brought forth Life. Moses foresaw you as a bush burning with fire, O Virgin Mother of God; therefore, extinguish the furnaces of my passions, and snatch me from the fire of Gehenna and save me. Virginity and childbirth concurred in you above nature, O ark of the venerable sanctification, O pure Virgin Mother. Therefore, I cry to you with faith: Sanctify me wholly, and deliver me wholly from every activity of the passions that trouble me. *From the Canon of Friday of the second week.* Let us honor the pure one with a pure mind; let us adorn the beauty of Jacob with divine actions, and let us piously praise her as the Mother of our God. O gate of God, closed, through which only the Lord passed, lead me along divine paths, and open for me the gates of salvation, O you who are most dear to God; for to you I flee, O Virgin, the unique protection of the human race. Spare me, O Christ, when you come to judge the world with glory, dissolve the fog of my evils through the intercessions of her who gave birth to you, and make me an heir of your heavenly kingdom. (47). That, in order for Mary to be a worthy seat of God, wherein he might dwell through the incarnation, she was required to be free from original sin, is demonstrated at length by Velasquez in his work *De Maria immaculata concepta*, book 1, dissert. 1, annotat. 3, which you should consult.
From the Canon on Saturday of the second week
O Maiden, give me strength that I may fast and abstain from every sin, you who ineffably gave birth in the flesh to the Almighty, the sinless Word, and grant me, O All-pure one, the cleansing tear for my pollutions. I offer a hymn to you, O Virgin; do not despise me, held fast by evils, but grant me, O chaste one, perfect correction through fasting and a virtuous manner of life. Ezekiel foresaw you, O chaste one, as the gate through which no one could pass, opening the gates of repentance to all who are in despair; wherefore I supplicate you, open to me the paths that lead to the rests thereof. Appear, O immaculate Lady, as a divine helper to your useless servants in the time of abstinence, presenting our prayers to the Lord, the King of the ages.
From the Canon of Monday of the third week, for Orthros
Hail, O most holy temple, the fleece bedewed by God, the fountain sealed of the immortal stream; protect your flock, O Lady, unassailed by all kinds of enemies. Having conceived the uncultivated cluster, restore me, darkened by the drunkenness of sin, to sobriety, O Theotokos Virgin, the hope of our souls. You are, O Theotokos, our armor and wall, you are the refuge of those who come unto you; we now move you to intercession that we may be delivered from our enemies. O full of divine grace, chaste and blessed, with the celestial powers and the archangels, and all the bodiless ones, intercede unceasingly for us all to Him who was born of you through compassion, that there may be given to us, before the end comes, forgiveness and propitiation of sins, and amendment of life, so that we may be able to find mercy.
From the Canon of Tuesday of the third week
Stretch forth your divine hands, O all-holy Virgin, with which you bore the Creator, who was incarnated out of kindness, and supplicate Him to deliver us from temptations, passions, and dangers—we who longingly praise you and cry out to you: Glory to Him who dwelt within you, glory to Him who went forth from you, glory to Him who delivered us through your birth-giving. Save me, save me, you who gave birth to the Savior; behold my affliction, O chaste one, which the multitude of my uncounted evils brings daily to my wretched soul, out of despair. O chaste Virgin Mary, supplicate the Judge whom you brought forth, in the hour of judgment, to take pity on me and save...
me, who am liable to condemnation; O sole protection of the human race.
The mountain untouched by the mason; the inaccessible gate, higher than the heavens and all created things; the beauty of Jacob; the all-golden urn, and the bridge; and the Mother of the Creator, the Virgin, let us, the faithful, bless.
From the Canon on Wednesday of the 3rd week, at Matins.
The Word became incarnate from you, O immaculate one, and came forth, that He might raise up the fall of the first parents through the bowels of His own mercy.
God’s Wisdom, building a house for Herself, became incarnate from you in an ineffable condescension of Her own, O unwedded Virgin; for you alone of all generations have been chosen, incorrupt, for the dwelling of the incorruptible Word.
From you, O Virgin, He who is rich by His own nature voluntarily accepted our poverty; and He who is invisibly praised by the choirs above became visible to us, restoring the shattered image through His own goodness.
From the Canon on Thursday of the 3rd week, at Matins.
Holy Lady, Mother of Christ our God; since you gave birth in an ineffable manner to the Maker of all; together with the holy apostles, entreat His goodness continually, that He may free us from perturbations of the soul, and grant us forgiveness of sins.
Extinguishable lamp, bridal chamber radiant on all sides, more sublime than the Seraphim, chariot of the Cherubim, all-immaculate one, deliver me from grievous sins and dangers.
Generations of generations bless you, as you formerly foretold, O most divinely blessed one: for you alone have brought forth to men the blessed Word, who in an inexplicable manner assumed a body from you.
Illumine me with divine light, O Virgin Theotokos, begetter of light; and dissipate the darkness of my soul, that I may bless you, whom all generations of men, just as you formerly prophesied, call blessed.
From the Canon on Friday of the 3rd week, at Matins.
You gave birth as a babe to the new Creator, O most chaste one; entreat Him, I pray, that He may deign to renew me, who am grown old in much malice.
Restrain, O Mother of Christ God, the malice of my heart, which is everywhere in jeopardy; shatter the darts of the demons, and the bows of those attacking my wretched soul.
Most holy Lady, my hope and my refuge, heal the wounds of my soul; bring peace to my mind, that I too, rejoicing, may praise your wonders, O Theotokos, ever-Virgin.
From the Canon for Saturday of the third week, at Matins.
That we may celebrate you with resonant voices, O Maiden, accept these our cries, and entreat your Son to grant us forgiveness of debts. You have brought forth, beyond the laws of nature, the Creator who became man, O immaculate one; therefore, supplicate Him now to overlook the iniquities and transgressions of all. As one who bore God, who is without sin, wipe away our sins by your motherly intercessions, O Theotokos, and save those who cry out: Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord. Your womb has become a tabernacle of light, O most holy, most pure one; wherefore I faithfully cry out to you: Irradiate the pupils of my soul, and show the straight path to him who genuinely calls you blessed.
From the double Canon for Tuesday of the fourth week, at Matins.
You carry in your womb, O Virgin, Christ who became flesh without change; entreat Him earnestly to deliver from carnal passions those who worship the cross which He endured in the flesh. Ineffable is the miracle of your conception, O Virgin Mother; for how do you conceive and yet remain chaste? How do you give birth to a child, yet remain completely ignorant of man? He alone knows it, who above nature was brought forth from me, being the Word of God, in a new manner. You bring forth, as a spiritual sanctuary, God who rests in the saints, O holy and chaste Mother of God: Him, I say, who today sanctifies all the ends of the earth by the divine adoration of the most holy cross, to which He was affixed in the flesh. You alone conceive without corruption, you alone suckle an infant, knowing naught of the pollutions of childbirth; you alone bring forth the Creator and your Master; you are both handmaid and Mother. We praise you, O Virgin parent, unto the ages. All generations call you blessed, O chaste one; for Jesus alone, He who was born of your womb, Himself did wonders with you, O Virgin Maiden, as He knows how; entreat Him for the salvation of His flock and His people. As your ewe-lamb stood beside the cross, O Jesus, seeing you, her Shepherd and Master, she wept bitterly, and, with her heart moved for you, O Christ, she cried out: What is this strange sight? How do you die, O Life?
From the double canon of Thursday of Mid-Lent, at Matins.
Maria, venerable receptacle of the Lord, raise up us who have fallen into the chaos of dire desperation, and of sins, and of tribulations; for thou truly art the salvation and help of sinners, and a mighty protection, and thou savest thy servants.
Mother of the good God, make my soul good, which the Wicked One has depraved by evil habit, wretchedly ensnaring me; so that I may always celebrate thee with praises as the cause of salvation, thou who art worthy of most abundant praise.
Thou hast borne, O Maiden without man, God—all-immaculate one—who holds the ends of the earth in his palm, and is more ancient than all things.
Not having abandoned the bosom of the Father, thou didst recline in the bosom of the Virgin, for the sake of the inclination toward the human race, the likeness of which thou didst assume because of the bowels of thy mercy: whence we men praise thee unto the ages.
Thou, who didst conceive joy at the voice of the archangel, abolish now the sorrow of my heart, which corrupts my soul; and give me, O chaste one, a mourning that brings forth joy, that I may find there the divine consolation.
Thee, the glorious and only Theotokos, who didst contain in thy womb the incomprehensible Word and didst bring him forth in the flesh, we magnify with hymns.
From the canon of Friday of Mid-Lent, for the adoration of the precious Cross.
From the canon of Saturday of Mid-Lent, for the adoration of the precious Cross.
"When I gave birth to thee, O Son, in an ineffable manner, I escaped the pains of child-birth; but how am I now filled with sorrows? For I see thee hanging like a malefactor upon the wood, thou who didst suspend the earth without any support," said the all-pure one, weeping.
Seeing Life put to death upon the Cross, and not being able to restrain the pain of her bowels, the venerable Virgin was shaken, exclaiming: "Woe is me, my Son, what has a people of prevaricators and iniquitous ones rendered unto thee?"
"From a virginal womb I bore thee, O Son, and seeing thee hanging upon the wood, I am utterly at a loss, and I do not comprehend the height of the mystery, nor the depth of thy many judgments," the all-pure one cried out, whom we, with incessant voices, beatify as the Mother of God.
Beholding upon the Cross Him for his goodness born of thee, O all-immaculate one, beyond understanding, thy bowels were wounded and thou didst say: "Woe is me, divine Son; how art thou pained for the sake of all? I adore thy compassion; glory be to thee, O Lord."
Grant us strength, O chaste one, that we may fast from all malice, and strengthen us to abstain always from depraved and evil actions, thou who existest as the protection of all men.
Expand the narrowness of my mind by thy intercession, O Lady, thou who hast narrowed all the paths of the stratagems of the enemy who attacks us, and [hast guided me] by a narrow path—
to the widening of life to walk, O Mother of God.
Now, as I behold thee, my eternal Son, suspended like an innocent lamb and affixed to the cross for the sake of the wicked, I am racked with lamentations and surrounded by motherly pains, cried out the all-pure one, whom we dutifully praise with unceasing voices unto all ages.
Without corruption I gave birth to thee from my womb, whom the Father begat before the ages; and how do corruptible men rend thee asunder, O Son, and pierce thy side with a lance, and inhumanly fasten thy hands and feet with nails? Thus the all-pure one cried out, whom we rightly magnify.
From the Canon of Saturday of the Mid-Week, at Matins.
The divine Word of God, seeking to deify the human race, O pure one, is incarnate of thee and appears as a mortal; beseech him without ceasing, that at the time of giving an account we may find mercy.
We beseech thee, the only good one, O Virgin, make us, who are infected with malice, good; and earnestly entreat Christ, who is good by nature, that we may complete the time of abstinence in good works, praising the same Christ.
When we behold the precious cross of Christ, O pure Theotokos, and worship it from the heart, make us through thy intercessions to the Master worthy to behold his venerable sufferings, having been purified from our own passions.
Mortify the movements of my flesh, thou who didst bring forth God in the flesh beyond the power of mind, and grant illumination to my intellect, O pure one, being a cloud of light.
From the Canon of the Second of the Fifth Week, at Matins.
Overcome by the beauty of thy virginity and the exceeding brightness of thy purity, Gabriel cried out to thee, O Theotokos: "What worthy encomium shall I offer thee? And by what name shall I call thee? I am at a loss and beside myself. Therefore, as I was commanded, I cry to thee: Hail, thou who art full of grace."
O Maiden worthy of many titles, hail, holy Virgin, Mother of God, Mary, the boast of the faithful, the redemption from the curse, the heavenly ladder, the miracle beyond understanding, the bush unconsumed, the earth never plowed.
O thou who remained incorrupt after childbirth, all-blameless, deliver thy servants, we pray, O pure one, from corruption, as we faithfully sing together in unity of soul: Bless the Lord, all ye works, etc.
O Christ, ever receive thy Mother, who entreats thee together with the martyrs, the prophets, and all the saints on behalf of thy servants, who have provoked thee, the only lover of mankind and Master, whom the heavenly hosts tremble before.
From the canon for Tuesday of the fifth week, at Matins.
Thou hast become the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only most chaste Virgin-Mother, having surpassed the angels in purity; cleanse me, who am defiled above all by carnal transgressions, by thy intercessions, O venerable one, granting through divine irrigations great mercy. Christ rested in thee, O only blessed one, and from thee took upon himself flesh. With the drops of thy compassions, O ever-virgin inviolate, wash away the passionate pollutions of my heart, granting to me channels of tears that offer psychic purification. Illumine my soul, darkened by the blackness of sensual passions, O chaste one, who gavest birth to the Light, that I may always magnify thee with faith.
From the canon for Wednesday of the fifth week, at Matins.
Seeing him on the wood, hanging in the midst of evildoers, he who had received flesh from thy pure blood and was born of thee in a way beyond understanding, O venerable one, thy inward parts were pierced, and with maternal affection thou didst lament and cry out: Woe is me, my Son! What is this thy divine and ineffable dispensation, through which thou hast brought thy creation to life? I praise the compassions of thy heart. O saving gate, bridge leading to God, protectress of Christians, most chaste Lady; guide me, O Maiden, who am surrounded by the calamities of life and tossed by its waves. O most chaste Mother of God, blot out the wounds of my soul and the scars of sin, drying them out and scorching them with the fountains flowing from the side of thy offspring, and purifying them with the streams that proceed from him; for to thee I raise my voice, and to thee I flee, and thee I invoke, O full of grace. Thou didst conceive, O Virgin, without seed him who created all things, the Word of God, without the wills of the flesh; and without corruption thou didst give birth, without the birth-pains of mothers; therefore, confessing thee with tongue and heart as Theotokos, we magnify thee.
From the canon for Friday of the fifth week, at Matins.
Seeing thee suspended on the Cross, O most holy one, she lamented, her heart pierced, and said: Because of the compassions of thy mercy, O Lord, thou didst deign to suffer, bestowing passionlessness upon all. We have known thee as the jar containing the divine manna of the Godhead, O Maiden; as the ark, the table, the lampstand, the throne of God, and the palace; as the bridge leading to divine life those who sing: Let all creation bless the Lord. O Lady, more sublime than all, make me superior to the malice of the passions, while I glorify thee as the true Theotokos, and celebrate thy birth-giving with hymns, O immaculate one, dearest to God, honored in incomprehensible fashion.
From the Canon of the Saturday of the Fifth Week, at Matins.
Thou who alone art worthy of all praise, pray for those who faithfully praise thee, that there may be granted to them the remission of sins and the distribution of divine gifts, and entreat the most holy Word.
Ever-virgin, who brought forth the Giver of the Law, entreat the same that He may remove all the iniquities of those who in this present time resolve to exercise themselves diligently in the divine fast.
By the supplications of her who bore thee, O Merciful One, and of thy holy martyrs and apostles, enlighten our souls that we may glorify thee in the exultation of the soul unto all ages.
At the voice of the angel, thou didst conceive ineffably the Angel of the great counsel of the Father, O Theotokos; therefore accept the voices of thy servants, which we offer in the time of the fast, and offer them to God, as incense.
From the Canon of Tuesday of Palm Week, at Matins.
The beginning of salvation was the address of Gabriel to the Virgin; for she heard "Hail," she did not flee from the greeting, she did not doubt as Sarah did in the tent, but she said thus: "Behold the handmaid of the Lord, be it unto me according to thy word."
To thee we cry, Rejoice, thou who didst conceive joy, O full of grace, Theotokos Virgin: entreat God, whom thou didst bring forth, that He may deliver from dangers and corruption those who assiduously celebrate thee.
O most holy Virgin, do thou save me and be the help of my infirmity, thou who didst conceive the Lover of mercy, whom we superexalt unto all ages.
From thee, O Virgin most dear to God, for those held in the night of the passions, there arose Christ, the Author of light and peace, washing away the transgression born of negligence, and clearly bestowing redemption upon us.
From the double Canon of Wednesday of Palm Week, at Matins.
Protected by the precious cross of thy Son, O chaste Lady Theotokos, we all easily turn aside every assault of the enemy; therefore we rightfully glorify thee as the Mother of Light and the only hope of our souls.
O chaste one, who wast in no wise consumed by the fire of the Divinity, burn up the material motions of my passions, that with faith, O chaste one, I may glorify thee.
O immaculate Virgin Mother, the rod of Jesse, from whom Christ arose, the life-giving Flower of earth-born men, through whom we were redeemed from corruption and mortality, all of us praise thee, O chaste one.
In thee, O chaste Virgin, were seen all the ways of God, who preserved thy purity clearly sealed after birth unto all ages.
Behold, all generations call thee blessed, O immaculate one, contemplating thy great deeds; for thou didst supernaturally bring forth the Maker of the whole world, being God and man.
Among women thou alone wast made beautiful, O most admirable and pure one, having conceived the most beautiful Word, who appeared more beautiful than all mortals. Entreat him, therefore, to beautify the ugliness of my heart, O Virgin.
Who has heard of a virgin giving birth, and a mother without a man? Thou accomplishest such a prodigy, Mary; but tell me, how? Do not search out the depths of my divine birth; this is true, but its comprehension is beyond the human mind.
From the Canon of Thursday of Palm Sunday, at Matins.
Heal swiftly the painful passions of my soul and the diseases of my flesh, O most pure one. Still the instability of my mind, and make me worthy, O Theotokos, to offer pure prayers to the King of all with tranquility of soul, and to beseech him for the remission of my sins.
From thee gushed forth the divine water of immortality and life, O most pure and chaste Mother of God, by which those who drink are delivered from death-bearing thirst.
The prophet saw thee as a bright gate of light, O most pure one; for thou didst bear the Giver of Light, who became like us in an ineffable manner, whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.
In two wills and essences thou broughtest forth for us the only-begotten Son of the Father, O immaculate Maiden, who became man, that the supreme God might make us partakers of the divine nature, O undefiled one.
From the Canon of Friday of Palm Sunday, at Matins.
O most pure Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou beheldest thy Son and God being crucified voluntarily, whom, O blessed one, cease not to entreat that he may grant us the forgiveness of our sins in the time of fasting.
Moisten with the drops of thy mercy, O most immaculate one, my heart melted by the flame of sin, and light the extinguished lamp of my heart, O gate of light.
The sacred voices of the prophets proclaim thee symbolically, O Virgin, as a gate, and a mountain, and a holy tabernacle, and a cloud of light, from which the sun, the only Giver of Light, arose for those sitting in darkness and in the shadow, O Virgin.
Tremendous is thy birth-giving, O Theotokos, Mother of Christ; therefore we faithfully call thee blessed, and we venerably glorify thee, generation after generation, unto the ages. Amen. C
